- `0a9d99d9ead8` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "pervasive_map_divergence" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his per-map M5 run (2026-08-31): the divergence is PERVASIVE, not localized - 41 of 48 StaticOnce maps, 26 of 28 DynOmnis maps and both content-bearing DynMeshes maps (53, 56) differ from the dev box; the few identical maps are the near-empty ones (hashes at/near the FNV basis signature). Every map with substantive rasterized content carries the LSB divergence, so map-level localization is EXHAUSTED and the parked +-1-ULP origin is confirmed to reach the shadow raster wholesale. The actionable lever is the AMPLIFIER: his m5_diag4 table shows --no-shadow_polyid alone restores the ceiling, i.e. the pure depth test reads LIT there - the polyId rescue is what hard-shadows a receiver when a CO-PLANAR different polygon owns the shadow-map texel (ownership flips freely under LSB noise). Robustness candidate: when polyIds differ but the stored depth is within a small guard band of the receiver depth (a co-planar occluder), treat as lit - byte-null to prove impossible (it changes a shadow decision), so gate: default-off flag, dev-box pins byte-identical with flag off, his M5 run + his eye judge the flag-on arm.
- `149c89a479b3` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "sph_differs" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his M5 run (2026-08-31): sph_differs - all three [SPH] invocations differ from the dev box, INCLUDING seq=0 StaticOnce (the one-shot deterministic static bake, 48 maps, 12.58M texels). The shadow MAPS themselves are machine-dependent at the packed depth+polyId plane level, so the divergence is inside the shadow RASTER, not the lookup; the ceiling defect is the polyId rescue failing against a map whose texels a different (co-planar) polygon owns on the M5. Next: per-map hashes to name WHICH light/face flips, then a texel-level plane diff for the mechanism.

### Disputed — counter-evidence or a human verdict disagrees (1)
- `6e87a0c3f0fb` measurement **platform.m5.greets_transparent_faces.dev_reference** = "f96caaf922e291dab771f8dfbfc485c2" (snapshot_md5) {defect=missing_polys scene=greets tool=m5} w=2/5 — The dev box renders his pinned pose (t=1043, FDS_GREETS_CAM=-0.822731495,2.76597214,-27.6483746,0.165945306,-0.243638918,0.955563784, the identical baseline judging arm, no guard, headless 1920x1080) with the transparent composite fully present: the smoked-glass banner pillars, the crab canopy, the Flood Greets holograms - colour dump md5 f96caaf922e291dab771f8dfbfc485c2. With his answer (missing in both arms on the M5, 9172282fb5e6) the defect is machine-bound and lives in or before the transparent composite. Trap for the hunt: the FDS_XPAR_TRACE census file is written only by the interactive F9 dump (DEMO/GREETS.CPP line 4279), NOT by the --snapshot path, so a headless M5 frame carries pixels but no composite census; the census needs an interactive F9 press with FDS_XPAR_TRACE=1 set. Next discriminator: his M5 snapshot of the same recipe (md5 + which elements are absent) and his F9 xtrace at the pose.

- `5452fa7984d7` measurement **flag.shadow_polyid.cli_effective** = "cli_effective" (state) {flag=shadow_polyid pose=conetest recipe=render_gate} w=3/5 — --no-shadow_polyid ON THE COMMAND LINE now takes effect; before b46ba7b5 it was INERT. g_shadowMode was a namespace-scope DYNAMIC initialiser reading FeatureFlags::shadow_polyid(), so it ran before main() parsed argv — the tree's only ERROR-severity semgrep finding (revival-featureflags-at-namespace-scope). Measured on the gate's own conetest recipe at 1920x1080 (--deferred --draw_cones --shadows --fast_fog --fast_fog_density=3): BEFORE, default and --no-shadow_polyid were both b41894f969d1f89dd2d7d794f160e286, bit for bit, while FDS_SHADOW_POLYID=0 gave be71c7ab18f23573daa2d1b9badc06fc. AFTER, default and --shadow_polyid stay b41894f969d1f89dd2d7d794f160e286 and --no-shadow_polyid gives be71c7ab18f23573daa2d1b9badc06fc, i.e. exactly what the env form always gave. Fix = a constant initialiser off FDS_SHADOW_POLYID_DEFAULT_ON plus one explicit Shadow_SeedModeFromFlags() called from REV.CPP right after FeatureFlags::parseArgs; NOT a lazy read, because g_shadowMode is loaded per shadow tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel. Default arm byte-null: render_gate ALL PASS 4/4, warm_gate fast ALL PASS, greets cam A t=5965 still dbe2d4c2716e2d762b366d5a45c151a6. tools/lint.sh after the fix: revival-featureflags-at-namespace-scope 0 findings, 0 at ERROR severity (the 102 raw-getenv WARNINGs are untouched), flag_readers 613/613 OK.

The surface is watertight everywhere EXCEPT along the T-junction host edges, where a hairline gap opens (the grey sky shows through). H6194: a white hairline the full height of the wall edge x=17.898 z=-62.952, the host edge of the reveal-head T-vertex (17.898,4.937,-62.952); that line projects through the engine camera model to frame x 1140-1153, the same line the shipping wedge (the 5273-px hole, 71704eb9e2b5) sits on. No wedge, no tab. line5: a dashed hairline down the crease at frame x ~1500, the census host line x=12.344 z=-24.687 (four T-vertices at y 2.469 / 3.086 / 6.789 / 7.406) projecting to x 1492-1509. H5981: no visible hairline (the host line z=-58.014 is seen edge-on). corner: clean, no census line in view. The unwelded 'authored' arm at H6194 reads the same as welded. - `09e57ac140d9` measurement **greets.displace.external_ref.blender.tjunctions_split** = "watertight_4_of_4_poses" (state) {arm=blender_tfix defect=wall detector=eye scene=greets state=tip51e59c09 (working tree: DEMO/MeshDump.{h,cpp}, greets_mesh_dump flag, GREETS.CPP dump calls + [GREETS-CAM] fov print, tools/blender_ref/, docs/evidence/blender_ref/; committed right after these records) tool=blender_ref} w=3/5 — Same pipeline, arm 'tfix': every welded vertex lying strictly inside another face's edge is split into that edge (bmesh edge_split + pointmerge, 38 splits in the iterative pass; the standalone census counts 40 (vertex, host edge) pairs on 17 host lines), BEAUTY re-triangulation, then the identical displacement. All four poses render watertight: H6194 no gap on the z=-62.952 line, the far wall's blocks turn into the reveal and the head course continues; line5 the dashed hairline is gone; H5981 and the 90 deg corner clean as in the welded arm. This is the literature rule (T-junctions before displacement) demonstrated on THIS mesh with THIS height map from HIS poses: with the topology fixed, smooth-normal displacement is watertight with no mitre, free-edge or border-pin stage. Cycles displaces each subdivided vertex once whichever face shades it, so the 0.42-tile u step across the reveal seam cannot open a gap, only put one side's relief on the shared line. NOT measured: what DisplaceStoneSubdiv does on a split mesh (approach A of the proposal page, unbuilt).

The vertex census over that line (FDS_STONE_CENSUS_BOX 17.5,18.3,-63.2,-62.7,4.5,6.4) shows pin 1 hCnt 0 at y=6.310/6.257/6.204 and 6.310/6.256/6.202 (two index families), pin 1 orig 1 at the authored y=4.937, free 1 from y=4.810 down - and NOTHING between 4.937 and 6.202. The far-wall sheet therefore bounds itself with ONE straight chord from the pinned (17.8980,6.2020,-62.9520) to the mitred (17.8400,4.8100,-62.9880) (moved 0.0683 u along -1x the doorway bisector (0.849,0,0.527)), while the spandrel bounds itself with the authored segment (17.8980,6.2040,-62.9520)-(17.8980,4.9370,-62.9520) which OWNS the y=4.937 vertex the far wall skips. Evaluating the chord at y=4.937 gives (17.84529,4.937,-62.98472): gap 0.0620 u, tapering to 0.0020 u at the top. Independently, lip-to-lip world separation reconstructed from the z16 over 724 rows reads min 0.0056 / p50 0.0306 / p90 0.0523 / max 0.1216 u against an amplitude range of +-0.164 u. - `8e5c22727eec` measurement **greets.displace.mitre.hole_share** = 1444 (hole_px) {arm=no_mitre defect=wall flag=greets_displace_mitre granularity=whole_frame metric=z16_zero_px pose=H6194 scene=greets} w=3/5 — The matched mitre weld is responsible for 3829 of the 5273 unrasterised pixels at H6194 but does not CREATE the crack. - `ec75f7f0d4d2` measurement **greets.displace.old_bake.h6194.junction_geometry** = "splayed_door_reveal_116deg_mitre_line_8" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip55772420} w=3/5 — WHAT MEETS AT H6194 (from the junction census with FDS_STONE_CENSUS_BOX=17.5,18.6,-63.6,-62.6,0,5.2 and the whole-scene [STONE-JUNC]/[STONE-MITRE] census): the room's west wall is the plane x=17.898 (authored face normal (-1,0,0)); it carries a doorway z in [-62.952,-58.014], y in [0,4.937]. The door reveal is SPLAYED - its faces have authored normal (-0.445,0,-0.896), so it leaves the corner line (x=17.898,z=-62.952) in direction (-0.895,0,+0.445): the wall and the reveal meet at a dihedral of about 116.4 deg, obtuse and convex from the room. That corner line is mitre line 8 of the bake (line 9 is the twin reveal at z=-58.014): w1 (+1,0,0) x129 faces, w2 (+0.445,0,+0.895) x126 faces, bisector (+0.850,0,+0.527), cosHalf 0.850, 105+105 welded verts spanning y 0.20..4.81. So this is NOT a room corner (those are the 90 deg concave welded ones) and NOT the 91.1 deg split seams: it is one of the ten mitre lines, i.e. the whole class 'high-angle junction' in this scene is the ten [STONE-MITRE] lines (4 sloped-vs-vertical near z=-22, 4 vertical with normals 63 deg apart at y 3.2-6.3, 2 door reveals). A closed, enumerable set. Instrument trap found on the way: the --displace_viz overlay (FDS/RENDER/WorldAabb.cpp DisplaceViz_DrawOverlay) back-face-culls with the AUTHORED face normal, so it draws no wireframe at all on walls whose winding is flipped (the x=17.898 wall renders displaced but shows nothing in the overlay).
- `73c046c6b83a` measurement **greets.displace.old_bake.h6194.missing_sheet** = "far_wall_border_chord_skips_the_spandrel_vertex_hinge_crack" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip55772420} w=3/5 — ANSWERED. The in-frame H6194 wedge is a T-JUNCTION HINGE CRACK between two faces of the SAME wall on the doorway jamb line (x=17.898, z=-62.952), not a gap between the wall and the splayed reveal. The far-wall sheet has no border vertex between the authored y=4.937 (pinned, orig) and y=6.202 (pinned), so its boundary there is ONE chord from (17.898,6.202,-62.952) to the mitred (17.840,4.810,-62.988); the spandrel above the door owns the y=4.937 vertex the far wall skips. Gap 0.062 u at the door head tapering to 0.002 u at the top (subagent:attack 087a63970e4c, from --face_id_dump + FDS_SNAPSHOT_ZDUMP world reconstruction, lip-to-lip p50 0.0306 / max 0.1216 u over 724 rows). 69.2% of the hole-boundary pixels have all neighbouring faces within 30 deg (64e87ccec15d): the splayed reveal bounds only ~88 of 509 boundary pixels. The coordinator's earlier box census (scratchpad oldbake/h6194_box/log.txt) shows the same geometry independently: [STONE-CORNERF] faces 5463 (pWp) and 5464 (ppW) span y 4.810..6.202 with their corner vert v6586 W moved (-0.058,0,-0.036) and the y=6.202 end unmoved, and [STONE-PROV] lists y=4.937 pin 1 orig 1 then y=4.810 free 1 with nothing between. CONSEQUENCE for his phrase 'where the high angle walls meet': the defect sits ON the high-angle jamb line, but the seam that tears is the coplanar one; the mitre widens it ~10x (5273 -> 1444 px without it, 8e5c22727eec) by moving the last vertex of one ladder where the neighbour has none. The double-valued profile (ad0b8faf2405) is real but is not what opens this wedge.
- `ad0b8faf2405` measurement **greets.displace.old_bake.mitre.profile_double_valued** = "union_profile_double_valued_sides_disagree_up_to_0.08u" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip55772420} w=3/5 — THE MITRE WELD IS NOT ONE CURVE. [STONE-FINALV] for the 210 verts on the H6194 corner line (x=17.898, z=-62.952): all 210 are WELD, all ride the same bisector (+0.850,0,+0.527), but the two sheets sample the height map in different UV columns (far wall u=-4.0774, reveal u=+0.5000), their verts are interleaved 1-4 mm apart in y, and MeshOps.cpp builds the shared profile as the UNION of both sides' samples (--greets_displace_profile_agree defaults to 0, so no collapse) - the profile is double-valued and each sheet effectively takes its own displacement: y=1.745 dsp -0.1296 vs -0.1973; y=1.808/1.811 -0.0406 vs -0.0904; and over the whole course y=1.9..3.2 the far wall reads +0.031 (stone, hEff 0.6) while the reveal reads -0.032..-0.055 (its column classed E, hEff 0.321) - the two welded polylines sit ~0.08 u apart for 1.3 u of height. The weld's watertight-by-construction argument (both polylines are re-samplings of ONE piecewise-linear curve) does not hold with a double-valued profile. CAVEAT, measured: inside the H6194 frame (world y ~3.5..6.4 visible) the polyline disagreement is at most 0.037 u (y=3.27: -0.138 vs -0.101; y=4.682/4.683: +0.009 vs -0.027) while the hole there is ~0.09 u wide, so the polyline mismatch alone does NOT account for the in-frame wedge; which sheet is missing there is open (subagent:attack asked to decode the z16 lips).

- `83aa7c0b6c4c` measurement **greets.displace.ref.crease_disagreement** = 21.2 (share_pct) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_crease_scan granularity=whole_frame metric=crease_length_unrelated_charts scene=greets} w=5/5 — The crease census over 453 plane-pair junctions, 7375 u of crease and 1085232 samples: length-weighted |d_self - d_neighbour| p50 = 0.0057 u, p90 = 0.0149 u, max 0.1731 u against the height field own |d|max of 0.1641 u. By crease LENGTH, 73.3% is phase-shifted (best-lag correlation >= 0.8, mean |lag| 1.39 texels - the same relief out of phase, which a dominant-owner rule genuinely reconciles), 5.5% partly related, and 21.2% (1560 u) is TWO UNRELATED CHARTS (best-lag correlation < 0.4) that no blend can reconcile. - `032cf9150e6a` measurement **greets.displace.ref.miss_cause** = 100.0 (share_pct) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim metric=miss_px_vetoed_by_trim pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5 — Every hole the reference renderer leaves is the MITRE TRIM applied where the survey rule does not reach. At cam A 4007 of 4009 miss pixels were vetoed by MitreTrimOk and NOT ONE of those vetoes came from a convex edge (1717 concave, 2290 soup-neighbour); at corridor t=5534, 8820 of 8946 with only 92 convex. The survey sentence is a CONVEX-corner rule; applied at every shared edge it turns the model union into an intersection, and against a SOUP neighbour it compares the point to the neighbour bare authored plane because there is no height field there.
- `383d12a718f2` measurement **greets.displace.ref.miss_px** = 773 (hole_px) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim granularity=whole_frame metric=miss_px pose=nine_pose_battery scene=greets} w=5/5 — Nine-pose battery of the corrected trim: the reference miss count falls from 31307 to 773 total (97.5%), with FIVE of nine poses going to exactly zero (camB, H6017, H6194, corner6097, curved2845). Grown pixels rise 42165 -> 46241 (+9.7%). Adding the dominant-edge arm on top makes the holes WORSE at five of nine poses (camA 36->46, camB 0->43, corridor 217->367), better at one and equal at three, so it does not earn the default on this number.
- `8ab9ddcb58e2` measurement **greets.displace.ref.miss_px** = 36 (hole_px) {arm=ref defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim metric=miss_px pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5 — Restricting the mitre trim to CONVEX edges (greets_displace_ref_mitre_trim=2, the new default) takes the reference miss count from 4009 to 36 at cam A and 8946 to 217 at corridor t=5534, while holding the grown-pixel count the trim exists to hold: 4075 -> 4368 and 140 -> 203. Trim off entirely gives the same 36 misses but 21634 grown pixels at cam A - the convex-corner spike the rule is for. Mode 3 (convex AND never against a soup neighbour) gives back 21529 of those, so the bare-plane stand-in is doing real spike control and mode 3 is refuted as a candidate.

- `a4378cff52a4` measurement **greets.displace.seam.phase_lever.adjacency** = "shared_vertex_adjacency_misses_t_junctions_collinear_overlap_sees_them" (state) {arm=uv_seam_phase scene=greets state=tipe24f1ca3 (working tree: SeamPhase.cpp, MeshOps.cpp [STONE-UVSEAM], LightmapBake.cpp 8-hue mode 2, Snapshot.cpp cycle harness; committed right after these records)} w=3/5 — TRAP (first two builds of --greets_uv_seam_phase, 2026-09-02): adjacency by SHARED VERTEX INDICES sees none of the authored stone's T-junctions - the door reveal's jamb edge runs door-height (y 0..4.937) against the far wall's full-height edge, the spandrel strips abut the wall quad mid-edge - so the jamb was never a constraint: the reveal and the far wall drifted apart (0.42 -> 0.33 tile) and two faces of ONE wall got different shifts, a new 0.26-tile step inside the far wall at x 17.898 z -62.95..-63.21 (fi0/fi11/fi44 against fi251/fi236/fi258 in the pixel census). Adjacency by COLLINEAR OVERLAP - two edges of different faces on one line (both endpoints within 2e-3 u of the other's line) overlapping over > 5e-3 u, each face's UV interpolated along its own edge at the overlap's ends - sees them: 290 seams against 84 shared edges, the jamb closes to 0.006 tile and the far wall moves as one patch. Patches are then the union over seams whose u AND v agree at both overlap ends (a crease already continuous in UV is one patch and can never be torn; the earlier coplanar-patch rule tore walls that bend up to 3.4 deg between faces).
- `34058c4a916f` measurement **greets.displace.seam.uv_step** = "base_mirUV_boundary_405_to_443_texels_every_other_seam_le_12" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip74d46abd (G-buffer dump rendered by the 2026-09-02 20:30 binary; the txtr/face/z16 planes are untouched by the later uv_viz commit)} w=3/5 — PER-PIXEL UV STEP ACROSS EVERY STONE SEAM ON SCREEN at H6194, decoded offline from the G-buffer dump (greets_t006194_mat.u32 txtr word -> texel column/row exactly as Render_UvViz does, verified against the on-screen --mat_probe readout at (960,540): 860,227 mip0 both ways; face owner from greets_t006194_face.u32; dihedral from the [FACEID] normals; world-adjacent only = |dz| < 0.05 u across the pixel pair so silhouettes are excluded). 83181 stone pixel pairs straddle a face boundary. SAME-MATERIAL seams, 82681 pairs: coplanar (<5 deg, n=36052) du p50 0 p90 3 max 10 texels; 5-30 deg (n=19388) p90 2 max 12; CREASE >=30 deg (n=27241) p90 2 max 10.5 - all UV-CONTINUOUS to within the in-face pixel gradient (p99 5 texels). The rooms|rooms::mirUV BOUNDARY, 500 pairs, ALL of them: du p50 404.5-405 p90 411-437 max 443 texels = 0.40-0.43 of the 1024 tile, dv <= 4 texels; fraction with du > 32 texels = 1.000 in both dihedral buckets (5-30: 198 pairs, >=30: 302 pairs), no coplanar base|mir pair is in view. Named: the door jamb x=17.9 z=-62.94 (rooms::mirUV u0=-4.077 vs rooms u0=+0.497, 434-438 texels, dihedral 48-79) and the pier at x~9.96 z~-59.0 (rooms u0=-0.819 vs rooms::mirUV u0=-3.422, 403-407 texels, dihedral 6-31, SHARED vertex A on both sides). So the UV discontinuity he sees is EXACTLY the handedness-clone boundary that GreetsFixBitangentHandedness (DEMO/GREETS.CPP:1524, --greets_tbn_fix) draws: it re-points every negative-UV-determinant face onto '<mat>::mirUV' and changes no UV, so the step is the AUTHORED projection phase between a mirrored sheet and its non-mirrored neighbour, ~0.4 tile in u at both places. Consequence for the bake: only these seams sample DIFFERENT height columns on their two sides (the double-valued profile ad0b8faf2405 is specific to them: mitre lines 8-9 and any other line crossing a handedness boundary); every same-family crease already samples the same column on both sides.

Gates that PASS: OFF arm byte-identical on all four rows, v4 arm 24/24 deterministic, tear 14 px of a 15 cap, slivers p10 2.41 (P2 2.26) with the same 8 sub-1deg faces, faces unchanged at 60606, bake 55.8 ms of a 500 ms budget. The cause is NOT the height rule and NOT the pinned rings: at --v4_cpb=1 the block interior carries no lattice node at all (mechanism 256d9f38b114), and the same height rule with a node in the block reads plateau -0.0084 and dz 0.74-0.78x the bare floor, PASSING the dz condition 4 of 4. What blocks the phase is the LATTICE, which is P2 s stage (c) and P7 s density dial, and the one lattice change measured here (--v4_band_union) opens 5 interior pinholes and breaches the face budget (d5b5dbb0f1d4). P4 must not start on this: the plateau the corner rule reconciles is 0.1 u out of place.

The wall is a RUNNING BOND: band k+1 s vertical mortar sits at the middle of band k s blocks. At --v4_cpb=1 the target cell IS the block pitch, so ceil(pitch/target)=1 and a block interior carries NO column line of its own -- the [V4-DISPLACE] census reads 8638 plateau nodes against 20308 groove nodes, and every one of them sits on a mortar pad line. The only nodes inside a band-k block come from the union ROW at a band change, where band k+1 s mortar columns are injected, and the row below has nothing under them; the Delaunay then covers the block with triangles whose three corners are ALL mortar-deep. Measured directly at cam A: a triangle at (153.25,127.5) (150.75,127.5) (126.75,189.75) texels with corner heights h=0.048/0.027/0.233, spanning a 57.5-texel block whose field reads h=0.59. Cross-tabulating the field class against the geometric class, the plateau-by-field / plateau-by-grid cell holds 149571 samples at e-r p50 -0.0799 u. This also settles a design premise: 2c s restricted-quadtree rule (adjacent cells differ by <=1 level) is NOT satisfied by the P2 lattice -- a 2.5-texel mortar cell abuts a 57.5-texel block cell, 4.5 levels apart -- and P2 s level_jump_violations=0 measured the row SPLIT COUNT, not the cell sizes.

Of 155 authored stone corners, 51 are solved and 104 are held: 48 because they sit on a use-count-1 ABUTMENT (a T-junction against a stone face the exact stitch cannot see, 418c3bf6c9d7 - the host has no vertex on the line, so moving this side alone opens the base junction bc79e39d), and 56 because the point coincides with a face of the same mesh this bake does not touch - measured by name, siling 50 and teleporter 6, which reproduces P1 own foreign-neighbour census (siling 50, teleporter 6) exactly. Of 1906 shared-border samples 1386 are solved, 246 held by an abutment and 274 held against a non-baked face. So the wall-to-wall creases DO now displace and the wall-to-CEILING boundary does not: moving a stone vertex away from an undisplaced siling face would tear that seam, which is a fact about the bake set, not about the ring solve. --v4_ring_abut is the measured arm that lifts the abutment half.
- `efef6e9e0158` measurement **greets.displace.v4.rings.residual** = 2.776e-17 (world_units) {arm=v4_p4 flag=v4_rings metric=ring_offset_plane_residual scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5 — The 2e ring invariant HOLDS EXACTLY where it can, and CANNOT hold where the design did not look. Every ring vertex is placed on the offset plane of every chart meeting it: over 1021 one-plane, 538 two-plane and 22 exactly-three-plane rings the max residual |n_i.(P-P0) - d| is 2.776e-17 u, against the 1e-5 u 2e asks - the closed forms are exact. But 12 ring vertices have FOUR OR MORE distinct chart planes, where the system is over-determined and has NO exact solution at all; 2e says 3-plane corner solve and does not cover them. Solving three exactly and ignoring the rest read 8.872e-03 u on the ignored planes; the least-squares point (NtN)x = d.Nt1 spreads it to 4.407e-03 u, which is what ships. Also measured: 0 mitre-limit clamps at limit 4, 0 singular (180 deg) folds, 0 ill-conditioned triples, max ring move 0.2055 u with d in [-0.1641, +0.0336].

- `0d44df27177b` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.asymmetry** = -0.2846 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_edge_inset_side metric=topleft_minus_bottomright_total_border_ao_loss pose=t5965-and-t5534 scene=greets} w=5/5 — RE-TESTED ON HIS EXACT VIEW CLASS AND IT STILL IS NOT TOP/LEFT - IF ANYTHING IT IS BOTTOM/RIGHT. Four content-matched probes (--ssao_edge_inset_side, the SAME pixels with a synthetic border 32 px to each side), including the corridor pose t=5534 whose LEFT WALL is grazing (|N.V| 0.27-0.47) and whose CEILING near the top of the frame is near-parallel (|N.V| 0.122) - the class the observation named. AO lift L/R/T/B: pose A centre 0.063/0.104/0.078/0.078; pose A top-left grazing wall (320,200) 0.043/0.197/0.152/0.075; corridor left wall (320,200) 0.027/0.015/0.001/0.090; corridor grazing ceiling (700,120) 0.027/0.051/0.016/0.044. The worst side tracks WHERE THE OCCLUDERS ARE, not the screen axis. The decisive form of the question - a corner is only ever exposed to ITS two borders - MIRRORED-CORNER TEST at identical inset magnitudes (288 px horizontal, 168 px vertical): pose A top-left loses 0.1942 AO total vs BOTTOM-RIGHT 0.4788; corridor top-left 0.0277 vs bottom-right 0.2977. In visible 8-bit levels on the lit frame the same ordering holds (A: 17.2 vs 36.8; corridor: 2.2 vs 33.5), and the bottom-right corner is the DARKER of the two (luma 49/63 vs 98/123), so it is not hidden by low contrast either.
- `c7e54ee64e11` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.asymmetry** = -0.0402 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_edge_inset_side metric=ao_lift_left_minus_right_at_k32 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE BORDER INFORMATION LOSS ITSELF IS NOT TOP/LEFT-BIASED. Content-matched test (--ssao_edge_inset_side puts a synthetic border k px to the LEFT of a chosen pixel in one run and k px to its RIGHT in another, so the SAME pixels are compared): at the frame centre column/row of cam A the AO lift is LEFT +0.0633 vs RIGHT +0.1035 at k=32 (right WORSE) and TOP +0.0775 vs BOTTOM +0.0784 (equal). At k=128: LEFT +0.0159 / RIGHT +0.0581, TOP +0.0124 / BOTTOM +0.0300. So which side reads worst is set by where the occluders happen to lie, not by the algorithm - the only mechanism with a genuine top/left sign is the march-offset truncation (see greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation), and that one is two orders of magnitude smaller.
- `a0fefb201622` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.resolution_dependence** = 12.6 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pp_swing_in_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — GTAO AO IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE SCENE - it moves with the pixel grid, and the hemisphere sampler does not. Same camera, same geometry, same flags, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed: GTAO ao<0.9 = 73.7 % at 1280x720, 66.5 % at 1920x1080, 61.1 % at 2560x1440 (meanAO 0.7732 / 0.7795 / 0.7928) - a 12.6-POINT swing from resolution alone. The --no-ssao_gtao hemisphere control at the same two of those resolutions gives 44.4 % and 44.3 % (meanAO 0.8778 / 0.8776), i.e. FLAT. Mechanism: the 256-px cap binds, so the world AO radius is cap*viewZ/FOVX, and FOVX is proportional to XRes - the radius therefore scales with depth AND inversely with horizontal resolution. This is the view-dependent shading Gil-Ad reported, and it is why his ladder cleared --no-ssao_gtao.

- `9f6575e68c03` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation** = 0.0044 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=rms_ao_delta_of_the_fix pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — REAL BUG, TOP/LEFT-SIGNED, SECOND ORDER. The GTAO march computed its sample pixel as `px + int(off + 0.5f)`, and C truncation toward zero makes that round-half-up ONLY for off >= -0.5; every negative offset lands ONE PIXEL SHORT. It does not average out over the rotations, because buildSliceTrig builds phi = (s + jit)*(PI/slices) with jit in [0,1], so phi never leaves [0,PI) and sin(phi) - the y component of every slice direction at all 16 rotations - is non-negative: every UP-going sample landed 1 px low and every LEFT-going sample 1 px right, while down/right were exact. Fixed by rounding to nearest (--ssao_gtao_round_fix, default ON, one instruction CHEAPER). Delta at cam A: rms 0.0044 AO, max 0.0908, 3.39 % of px move >0.01; asymmetric in the predicted direction - top 256-px band mean -0.00185 (6.82 % of px >0.01) vs bottom +0.00186 (1.12 %), left -0.00140 (4.24 %) vs right -0.00068 (0.03 %).
- `bfd122c99a15` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.srad_clamp** = 99.99 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pct_covered_px_where_cap_binds pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=3/5 — The GTAO march hard 256-px screen-radius cap (FDS/RENDER/DeferredSSAO.cpp#gtaoCell / #gtaoRow8: srad = ssao_radius*FOVX/z clamped to [2,256]) BINDS on 92-100 % of covered pixels at the greets review poses, so the AO scale in this scene is set by the cap, not by --ssao_radius. From the --ssao_dump view-Z plane at 1920x1080 (FOVX=1728, --ssao_radius=4, the zfloor inert in greets) the unclamped srad median is 894 px at t=5965, 1034 at t=5743 and 9333 at t=6097, and the cap binds on 99.99 / 92.12 / 100.00 % of covered pixels.
- `5f95e8304799` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.edge_band** = -53.0 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=pct_change_in_border_ao_error_0_to_32px pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — The front-loaded ladder roughly HALVES the border error in the strip where he said it is most jarring, and pays a little for it further in. Content-matched (--ssao_edge_inset=256, cam A 1920x1080), mean AO error by distance inward from the synthetic border, averaged over all four sides: 0-16 px +0.0716 -> +0.0404 (-44 %), 0-32 px +0.0640 -> +0.0299 (-53 %), 0-64 px +0.0438 -> +0.0225 (-49 %), 64-128 px +0.0105 -> +0.0118, 128-256 px +0.0036 -> +0.0058. The band-vs-interior gap on the untouched frame goes +0.0226 (uniform/256) to -0.0015 (geometric/1024) at pose A. CAVEAT STATED BECAUSE IT MATTERS: a 256-px inset fully accounts for a 256-px march but only partly for a ~894-px one, so the geometric arm's TOTAL border sensitivity is UNDER-measured by this instrument; the near-edge profile is the comparable statistic, the whole-frame mean is not (0.1200 vs 0.1249 is instrument, not physics).
- `846db4d1125f` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.frame_ms** = 0.12 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta pose=t5743 scene=greets} w=5/5 — COST-NEUTRAL, and the SSAO wave itself is slightly CHEAPER. greets t=5743, 1920x1080, --deferred_prof=5 --bench iters=40, 11 INTERLEAVED runs per arm on ONE binary, wall_min: renderFrame 49.792 (uniform/256) vs 49.853 (geometric/1024), +0.061 ms = +0.12 %, inside the +/-1.5-2 % placement floor; the ssao wave 7.140 -> 6.853 (-4.02 %) and ssao-march 5.007 -> 4.728 (-5.57 %), with the medians agreeing in sign (renderFrame median is LOWER for the geometric arm, 50.336 vs 51.482). Mechanism for the march being cheaper rather than dearer: the step count is unchanged, but a ~894-px march puts more samples OFF-FRAME, and the off-frame path skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build - and a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit. The per-cell ladder constants cost two quadratic minimax evaluations, not libm.
- `e279eac2ea39` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.near_step_artifacts** = 0.03 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024 flag=ssao_gtao_thick_clamp metric=pct_fully_black_px_in_the_saw_tooth_column pose=t5726 scene=greets} w=5/5 — BOTH OF HIS ARTIFACTS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS NOT A DEPTH-DISCONTINUITY BUG. Traced at t=5726 px(1129,225): the black saw-tooth column is NOT the near wall occluding the far background - those samples are correctly rejected (OUT-OF-RANGE, |d| = 31.5 against a 4 u radius). The black pixels are ON THE NEAR WALL, grazing, just inside its own silhouette, occluding THEMSELVES. Two guards were missing because the uniform ladder put the first sample 48-192 px out and never needed them. (a) THICKNESS: the bitmask back face is d - V*thickness, a flat world constant, so when |d| is much smaller than thickness it lands BEHIND the receiver and acos(dot(b,V)) saturates at PI - in the trace one sample at |d| = 0.066 set 11 of 32 sectors and one at 0.285 set THIRTY, and the saturation vanishes at the first step whose |d| (1.274) exceeds thickness (1.0). Fixed by --ssao_gtao_thick_clamp: back face bounded by min(thickness,|d|). (b) SELF-OCCLUSION: --ssao_bias is read ONLY by the hemisphere sampler, so GTAO shipped with no bias at all; at ~5 px the ZPage16 quantum (0.0025 u) and the half-res cell offset become a horizon at grazing incidence. Fixed by --ssao_gtao_bias (plane distance, AUTO 0.05 u). RESULTS at 1512x848 on his exact arm: t=5726 column fully-black fraction 32.90 % -> 0.03 % (std 0.343 -> 0.216); t=5836 column 8.23 % -> 0.00 % (std 0.280 -> 0.148, the shipped uniform arm is 0.147); t=5836 near-wall banding std 0.1462 -> 0.0725, BELOW the shipped uniform arm's 0.0796. Isolation: bias alone leaves 11.58 %/1.36 % black (the clamp owns the columns), clamp alone leaves the banding at std 0.139 (the bias owns the banding). --ssao_downscale=1 changes neither (37.59 %/9.86 % black), so the bilateral upsample is NOT implicated - that hypothesis is refuted.
- `6c0f5448991c` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.resolution** = 0.47 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=pp_spread_of_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560_at_cap4096 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE STRUCTURAL FIX WORKS: front-loaded march steps plus a cap large enough to stop binding collapse GTAO's resolution dependence to the hemisphere sampler's level. Same camera, same geometry, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed; ao<0.9 spread over 1280x720 / 1920x1080 / 2560x1440. Pose A: uniform+cap256 12.4 pp -> geometric+cap512 3.5 -> +cap1024 3.3 -> +cap4096 (cap binds 0.00 %) 0.47 pp, against the hemisphere control's 0.2 pp. Pose B: 8.6 -> 5.1 -> 1.7 -> 0.21 pp, hemisphere 0.5. meanAO at pose A goes 0.7729/0.7793/0.7925 (spread 0.0196) to 0.7780/0.7800/0.7821 (spread 0.0041). Pose C does NOT converge (5.9 -> 5.2 pp at cap 1024, 5.6 at cap 4096) and it is not a failure of the lever: at view-Z median 0.74 the authored 4-unit radius subtends more than the whole frame, the cap still binds on 84-99 % of pixels, and no screen-space method can carry that radius there. GEOMETRIC SPACING IS WHAT MAKES THE RAISED CAP USABLE: at cap 1024 the four steps land at 5.1/26.1/133.2/681.0 px instead of uniform's 192/448/704/960, and uniform at the same cap costs +0.0290 AO of interior contact occlusion where geometric costs +0.0152.
- `ab38cdd47b7e` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.resolution** = 0.0059 (world_units) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024-guards flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=meanAO_spread_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE GUARDS DO NOT REGRESS THE RESOLUTION RESULT - THEY EXTEND IT TO THE ONE POSE THAT HAD REFUSED TO CONVERGE. meanAO spread over 1280x720 / 1920x1080 / 2560x1440 with the guards on: pose A 0.0059, pose B 0.0030, pose C 0.0010, against the shipped uniform arm's 0.0196 / 0.0192 / 0.0019 and the unguarded geometric arm's 0.0017 / 0.0046 / 0.0556. Pose C is the win: its self-occlusion was itself resolution-dependent, so removing it collapses a 0.0556 spread to 0.0010. ao<0.9 spreads are 2.6 / 4.5 / 0.8 pp against the shipped 12.4 / 8.6 / 5.9. The BORDER number gives some back: content-matched (--ssao_edge_inset=256, cam A) the near-edge AO error at 0-32 px is +0.0640 shipped, +0.0299 unguarded geometric, +0.0453 with the guards - still 29 % better than shipped, no longer 53 %.

- `d585fec1a494` measurement **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.causal_ab** = "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) {arm=idshift_unguarded defect=ceiling_colour pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=2/5 — THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. THIS RECORD'S VALUE IS THE UNGUARDED REPRO SNAPSHOT md5, 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, whose ceiling band (rows 5..40, cols 600..1300) reads 82.8/63.4/47.6 against the M5's measured 85.3/65.8/47.8. The other three arms of the same battery: shipping d92cb6f5eb19da4301588ae83af6a56e = 85.2/133.5/139.0; idshift + --shadow_coplanar_guard 8dbbafb77629598e5a3e2b1bfaae9e1a = 85.1/133.8/139.3, i.e. with EVERY identity rescue in the scene destroyed the guard alone carries the frame back to within 0.3/255 of the shipping look; idshift + guard + --shadow_coplanar_guard_onesided bc7a68cc2e5eeec1ca4ab8ef350b5658, which is --no-shadow_polyid BYTE FOR BYTE (so requiring the depth test to agree degenerates to depth mode and the SYMMETRIC band is the actual guard). The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies.

- `ad6b638504d1` measurement **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.on_arm_cost** = 19.92 (share_pct) {arm=guard_on metric=px_changed_vs_off pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — WHAT THE FLAG-ON ARM COSTS ON THE DEV BOX, MEASURED not assumed. tools/render_gate.sh under FDS_SHADOW_COPLANAR_GUARD=1 is still 4/4 PASS with all four rows BYTE-IDENTICAL - those scenes have no co-planar shadow-group conflict. greets does, and it moves a lot. Same recipe, OFF -> ON: cam A t=5965 413,078 px differ (VALUE = 19.92%% of frame), 124,786 px >8, max delta 136, signed mean +4.63, 412,590 brighter vs 477 darker; default-camera t=1588 789,885 px (38.09%%), 155,842 >8, max 120, mean +3.69; t=2845 558,382 px (26.93%%), 292,069 >8, max 140, mean +12.07; t=5965 304,548 px (14.69%%), 99,823 >8, max 138, mean +4.75. DIRECTION: >=99.6%% of every changed pixel is BRIGHTER - the guard removes shadow and never adds any, which is what it is built to do, but on greets it removes a great deal. Whether that is wrongly-cast self-shadow (correct) or real contact shadow (a look regression) is HIS eye's call, not these counters'. PERF, single run --deferred_prof=1 at cam A: DeferredLighting-call 22.133 -> 24.697 ms (+2.56 ms, +11.6%%), lighting-w1 17.503 -> 20.002 ms (+2.50 ms), lighting-w2 2.549 -> 2.645 ms; most of it is the disarmed --deferred_cube_prepass, whose cached tail is called with lz=0 and so cannot serve the guard. Default stays OFF.

- `10e91a31d5d8` measurement **platform.m5.divergence_mechanism** = "fpcr_ah_set_by_fpu_lprecision" (state) {tool=m5} w=4/5 — ROOT CAUSE of the M5 divergence, same binary and assets on both machines: FDS/Base/FDS_VARS.H FPU_LPrecision() wrote FPCR = FZ|AH (0x1000002) on every thread that calls it (REV.CPP, MainLoop, Snapshot, SDL2, and every render worker via GENERAL.H). AH (bit 1) is ARMv8.7 FEAT_AFP 'alternate handling'. The M2 Max lacks FEAT_AFP: the bit is RES0, the write is silently dropped, workers read back 0x1000000 (AH=0) - plain FZ, denormal inputs AND outputs flushed - and that is the mode every pin was measured under. The M5 Max implements FEAT_AFP and honours the bit: workers read 0x1000002 (AH=1), which changes (a) FNEG/FABS - a NaN's sign bit is left unchanged, (b) FZ flushes outputs only, denormal inputs pass, (c) FRECPE/FRSQRTE precision (FEAT_RPRES), (d) FMIN/FMAX NaN propagation. Probe (scratchpad fpprobe/ahprobe.c): on the M5 AH=1 -> fneg(0x7FFFFFFF7FFFFFFF) UNCHANGED, AH=0 -> FLIPPED; the M2 cannot set the bit; fresh processes and threads are FPCR=0 on both, so the demo's own write is the only source. (a) alone is the transparent-panel / spire-orb defect: clang emits g_rasterXExtent={INT32_MAX,-1} as mvni.2s+fneg+str (a NaN bit pattern negated), so under AH=1 the extent's hi stays INT32_MAX and every xpar composite range is empty (raster_extent_bound_px, composite_filled_px). FIX: FPU_LPrecision now writes base|FZ (AH and FIZ cleared), reason in the comment. RESULT with the fixed binary (dev build md5 fe514908..., shipped to the M5 as Runtime/DEMO_dev, install_name_tool to /opt/homebrew/opt/sdl2 + ad-hoc codesign): greets t=1043 M5 cbe52ab0... -> f96caaf922e291dab771f8dfbfc485c2 = dev; fountain t=500 M5 048f3905... -> 4d62d5818c8b030061081e6c828e5547 = dev; fountain sweep t=100/300/500/800/1200 identical 5/5 (1d6c31fe/ba375e66/f8a20833/0b0fe2f9/d7efd097); tools/render_gate.sh on the M5 4/4 at the dev hashes (4ac809e5/826c09e6/b41894f9/166fa25a, where the M5 used to read 4043aaaa/88dd4632/ffa28566/1a98cdf6); tools/ovec/gates.sh on the M5 11 of 12 rows equal to the dev pins (greets t=1588, the four greets-acc poses 08f0741f/84e18552/0cc05784/456eac7d, fountain 0222f903, chase x7, three city-acc arms). REMAINING: the plain city row (FDS_CITY_ENV_PIXEL=1 city@t=1961 --deferred) reads 0debc80a on the M5 vs bd4ffbf8 on dev - deterministic on both, unchanged by an empty cache dir and by FDS_THREADS=1 on both, 3.358% of pixels differ, 0.004% by more than 8, max 18, bbox x[0,1687] y[0,641] - a small last-ulp-class residual, filed separately (platform.m5.city_residual). Dev is byte-null under the fix: render_gate 4/4, ovec 12/12, greets t=1043 and fountain t=500 unchanged.
- `172cb8803514` measurement **platform.m5.fountain.spire_orbs** = "byte_identical_to_dev_after_ah_fix" (state) {defect=missing_polys scene=fountain tool=m5} w=3/5 — The flat white parallelograms in the spire cages were the transparent layer (nested f_sphere glass shell + fiery in-shpere core) left UN-COMPOSITED because every clump's raster x-extent read back empty under FPCR.AH=1 (platform.m5.divergence_mechanism). - `eabfa0a7047e` measurement **platform.m5.greets_transparent_faces.raster_extent_bound_px** = 0 (count) {defect=missing_polys detector=xpar_extent_census pose=t1043 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — The number stands (154 of 154 clumps report an EMPTY raster extent on the M5, same binary, old FPCR) but the thread-local-instance hypothesis is REFUTED: the address prints show the raster ([XT-RAST] in FDS/FILLERS/Mekalele.h) and the read-back ([XT-EXT] in FDS/RENDER/DeferredSurfaceKernel.cpp) on the SAME pthread and the SAME &g_rasterXExtent / &g_rasterStripClamp on both machines, and a two-TU inline-thread_local test (scratchpad tlstest) is correct on both. What differs is the VALUE the arming store leaves behind: the M5 reads back hi=2147483647 where dev reads hi=-1 (lo=2147483647 on both). Mechanism (sibling record platform.m5.divergence_mechanism): clang materialises g_rasterXExtent={INT32_MAX,-1} as `mvni.2s v0,#0x80,lsl#24 ; fneg d0,d0 ; str d0,[x0]` - a NaN bit pattern negated - and under FPCR.AH=1 (written by FPU_LPrecision on every worker, honoured by the M5's FEAT_AFP, RES0 on the M2) FNEG leaves a NaN's sign bit alone, so hi never becomes -1, the raster's max-update never fires, cx1=(hi+1)<<3 overflows and every composite range is empty. In-demo readout at greets t=1043: M5 workers fpcr=0x1000002 AH=1 hi=2147483647; dev workers fpcr=0x1000000 AH=0 hi=-1. With AH cleared the M5 workers read fpcr=0x1000000 AH=0 and hi=-1 after the arm, and the extent bound is non-empty again (frame md5 = dev).

- `dad4de8f8962` measurement **platform.m5.polyid_has_no_depth_term** = "identity_only_no_bias" (state) {defect=ceiling_colour scene=greets state=mechanism_2026_08_31 tool=m5} w=1/5 — MECHANISM CONFIRMED FROM THE CODE (docs/SHADOW_GUARD.md §1): ShadowMode::PolyId decides occlusion by IDENTITY ALONE - `occ += w iff c != 0 && c != receiverId` in CubeShadow_Tail (FDS/FILLERS/ShadowMap.h), with NO depth term and NO bias; DeferredShadowSampling.h passes constBias=0/slopeBias=0 into it on purpose. Depth mode's constBias(512)+slopeBias(<=4096) IS the acne rescue and it is OWNERSHIP-BLIND, so a co-planar occluder reads LIT whoever wrote the texel - which is exactly why --no-shadow_polyid restores the M5 ceiling while the default shadows it. PolyId's only rescue is id equality, so a different-shadow-group co-planar neighbour hard-shadows the receiver with ZERO depth separation from its own plane. Ownership is decided by `enc > z_existing` in ShadowBarry, i.e. by ONE quantum, and the M5's maps diverge on 41/48 static maps. SHARPENING of the parked hypothesis: the id is a MATERIAL/shadow-group id (Material::ShadowMatID, else ID+1), NOT a per-polygon id, so same-group co-planar polygons never fight - and greets allocates 2183 per-plane-cluster shadow groups by design ([PIRAMID-SPLIT] census), which is 2183 chances for the mismatch. The predicate is copied in FOUR places: the cube tail, LightmapBake.cpp SampleStaticCubeAtWorld (the path greets STATIC omnis actually take), the spot tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel.cpp, and the cached tail in CubeTapPrepass.h.
- `0a9d99d9ead8` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "pervasive_map_divergence" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his per-map M5 run (2026-08-31): the divergence is PERVASIVE, not localized - 41 of 48 StaticOnce maps, 26 of 28 DynOmnis maps and both content-bearing DynMeshes maps (53, 56) differ from the dev box; the few identical maps are the near-empty ones (hashes at/near the FNV basis signature). Every map with substantive rasterized content carries the LSB divergence, so map-level localization is EXHAUSTED and the parked +-1-ULP origin is confirmed to reach the shadow raster wholesale. The actionable lever is the AMPLIFIER: his m5_diag4 table shows --no-shadow_polyid alone restores the ceiling, i.e. the pure depth test reads LIT there - the polyId rescue is what hard-shadows a receiver when a CO-PLANAR different polygon owns the shadow-map texel (ownership flips freely under LSB noise). Robustness candidate: when polyIds differ but the stored depth is within a small guard band of the receiver depth (a co-planar occluder), treat as lit - byte-null to prove impossible (it changes a shadow decision), so gate: default-off flag, dev-box pins byte-identical with flag off, his M5 run + his eye judge the flag-on arm.
- `149c89a479b3` measurement **platform.m5.shadow_plane_hash** = "sph_differs" (state) {detector=sph pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — ANSWERED by his M5 run (2026-08-31): sph_differs - all three [SPH] invocations differ from the dev box, INCLUDING seq=0 StaticOnce (the one-shot deterministic static bake, 48 maps, 12.58M texels). - `9b1594694cbb` measurement **platform.m5.speckle_mechanism** = 5.6e-05 (world_units) {arm=bare metric=clip_reconstruction_disagreement pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — NAMED the +/-1-quantum z16 speckle amplifier: the near-plane clip at Scene NZP=0.01 multiplies every clipped vertex screen coordinate by iNearZ=100, so a near-straddling face is carried through the 2D clip as coordinates of magnitude 1e6 (measured on the greets floor face: PX=-1726688.62 / +1132107.12, PY=514363.34 at RZ=100 against the un-clipped A at PX=2228.13, RZ=0.0899). ### Hypotheses — no falsifier stated (5)

### Floating premises — laws/traps a current record rests on, that rest on no measurement (4)
- `420fcd4626b8` law **displace.law.direction_and_shading_separate** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — The displacement direction is a separate field from the shading normal: on a planar patch it is the face normal (nothing to pollute); the shading normal is n ~ N - grad_s h in the plane frame, exact on a plane, with split normals at creases (dihedral threshold) and never an average across a crease.
- `f713599ea11d` law **displace.law.mitre_offset_planes** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — At a crease, displace the crease point by solving the two offset planes, u = d(n1+n2)/(1+n1.n2) (3x3 with the edge direction for unequal offsets) - never a normalised bisector. Sign-free at reflex corners; the only degeneracy is anti-parallel outward normals (a razor fin, material dihedral -> 0), handled by a mitre limit (SVG default 4). Every corner in greets caps at 2x the height.
- `7453a529070e` law **displace.law.tjunctions_before_displacement** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5 — T-junctions and duplicate border vertices must be eliminated BEFORE displacement: a shared-border vertex is evaluated once and indexed from both sides (R1), the border owns its sample count (R2), border parameters are exact i/n (R3). Displacing a T-vertex by its own sampled height turns a sub-pixel raster crack into a full-amplitude hole.
- `e43c035dbedf` trap **greets.displace.ref.bisector_partition_deletes_material** = "trap" (state) {defect=wall detector=refdiff flag=greets_displace_ref_partition pose=camA scene=greets} w=3/5 — The LITERAL candidate definition - the nearest base plane governing MEMBERSHIP - does not merely castellate, it deletes material: wherever the governing face height says air while a neighbour slab says solid, the point is air. A plane bisector is a NON-LOCAL partition, so a face seen edge-on has its plane nearest along an entire ray. Traced at cam A t=5965 px(1240,300): edge-on face 881 governs from t=5.58 while the pier own front faces begin at t=5.90, deleting the pier front surface over a 90 px band. The survey records that no source defines the bisector-partitioned union.

CHASE PAYS NOTHING FOR THE FLIP, AND ITS SSAO ROWS GET CHEAPER. chase renders TWO tagged frames per tick and both are flat: renderFrame@main 19.630 -> 19.634 ms (+0.02 %), renderFrame@refl 16.601 -> 16.649 (+0.29 %) - both an order of magnitude inside the +/-1.5-2 % floor. The SSAO rows move the OPPOSITE way to greets: ssao@main -0.99 %, ssao@refl -1.72 %, ssao-march@main -1.77 %, ssao-march@refl -2.67 %, ssao-blur -2.5/-3.3 %. Mechanism consistent with the greets record: with the cap at 1024 the march is ~4x longer in screen space, so far more samples land OFF-FRAME, and an off-frame sample skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build (a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit). In greets' tight interior that saving is outweighed by the guards; in chase's open corridor it wins.

### displace
- `420fcd4626b8` law **displace.law.direction_and_shading_separate** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — The displacement direction is a separate field from the shading normal: on a planar patch it is the face normal (nothing to pollute); the shading normal is n ~ N - grad_s h in the plane frame, exact on a plane, with split normals at creases (dihedral threshold) and never an average across a crease.
- `f713599ea11d` law **displace.law.mitre_offset_planes** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — At a crease, displace the crease point by solving the two offset planes, u = d(n1+n2)/(1+n1.n2) (3x3 with the edge direction for unequal offsets) - never a normalised bisector. Sign-free at reflex corners; the only degeneracy is anti-parallel outward normals (a razor fin, material dihedral -> 0), handled by a mitre limit (SVG default 4). Every corner in greets caps at 2x the height.
- `7453a529070e` law **displace.law.tjunctions_before_displacement** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating floating_premise] — T-junctions and duplicate border vertices must be eliminated BEFORE displacement: a shared-border vertex is evaluated once and indexed from both sides (R1), the border owns its sample count (R2), border parameters are exact i/n (R3). Displacing a T-vertex by its own sampled height turns a sub-pixel raster crack into a full-amplitude hole.

- `09e57ac140d9` measurement **greets.displace.external_ref.blender.tjunctions_split** = "watertight_4_of_4_poses" (state) {arm=blender_tfix defect=wall detector=eye scene=greets state=tip51e59c09 (working tree: DEMO/MeshDump.{h,cpp}, greets_mesh_dump flag, GREETS.CPP dump calls + [GREETS-CAM] fov print, tools/blender_ref/, docs/evidence/blender_ref/; committed right after these records) tool=blender_ref} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — Same pipeline, arm 'tfix': every welded vertex lying strictly inside another face's edge is split into that edge (bmesh edge_split + pointmerge, 38 splits in the iterative pass; the standalone census counts 40 (vertex, host edge) pairs on 17 host lines), BEAUTY re-triangulation, then the identical displacement. All four poses render watertight: H6194 no gap on the z=-62.952 line, the far wall's blocks turn into the reveal and the head course continues; line5 the dashed hairline is gone; H5981 and the 90 deg corner clean as in the welded arm. This is the literature rule (T-junctions before displacement) demonstrated on THIS mesh with THIS height map from HIS poses: with the topology fixed, smooth-normal displacement is watertight with no mitre, free-edge or border-pin stage. Cycles displaces each subdivided vertex once whichever face shades it, so the 0.42-tile u step across the reveal seam cannot open a gap, only put one side's relief on the shared line. NOT measured: what DisplaceStoneSubdiv does on a split mesh (approach A of the proposal page, unbuilt).

The door reveal is SPLAYED - its faces have authored normal (-0.445,0,-0.896), so it leaves the corner line (x=17.898,z=-62.952) in direction (-0.895,0,+0.445): the wall and the reveal meet at a dihedral of about 116.4 deg, obtuse and convex from the room. That corner line is mitre line 8 of the bake (line 9 is the twin reveal at z=-58.014): w1 (+1,0,0) x129 faces, w2 (+0.445,0,+0.895) x126 faces, bisector (+0.850,0,+0.527), cosHalf 0.850, 105+105 welded verts spanning y 0.20..4.81. So this is NOT a room corner (those are the 90 deg concave welded ones) and NOT the 91.1 deg split seams: it is one of the ten mitre lines, i.e. the whole class 'high-angle junction' in this scene is the ten [STONE-MITRE] lines (4 sloped-vs-vertical near z=-22, 4 vertical with normals 63 deg apart at y 3.2-6.3, 2 door reveals). A closed, enumerable set. Instrument trap found on the way: the --displace_viz overlay (FDS/RENDER/WorldAabb.cpp DisplaceViz_DrawOverlay) back-face-culls with the AUTHORED face normal, so it draws no wireframe at all on walls whose winding is flipped (the x=17.898 wall renders displaced but shows nothing in the overlay).
- `73c046c6b83a` measurement **greets.displace.old_bake.h6194.missing_sheet** = "far_wall_border_chord_skips_the_spandrel_vertex_hinge_crack" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip55772420} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — ANSWERED. The in-frame H6194 wedge is a T-JUNCTION HINGE CRACK between two faces of the SAME wall on the doorway jamb line (x=17.898, z=-62.952), not a gap between the wall and the splayed reveal. The far-wall sheet has no border vertex between the authored y=4.937 (pinned, orig) and y=6.202 (pinned), so its boundary there is ONE chord from (17.898,6.202,-62.952) to the mitred (17.840,4.810,-62.988); the spandrel above the door owns the y=4.937 vertex the far wall skips. Gap 0.062 u at the door head tapering to 0.002 u at the top (subagent:attack 087a63970e4c, from --face_id_dump + FDS_SNAPSHOT_ZDUMP world reconstruction, lip-to-lip p50 0.0306 / max 0.1216 u over 724 rows). 69.2% of the hole-boundary pixels have all neighbouring faces within 30 deg (64e87ccec15d): the splayed reveal bounds only ~88 of 509 boundary pixels. The coordinator's earlier box census (scratchpad oldbake/h6194_box/log.txt) shows the same geometry independently: [STONE-CORNERF] faces 5463 (pWp) and 5464 (ppW) span y 4.810..6.202 with their corner vert v6586 W moved (-0.058,0,-0.036) and the y=6.202 end unmoved, and [STONE-PROV] lists y=4.937 pin 1 orig 1 then y=4.810 free 1 with nothing between. CONSEQUENCE for his phrase 'where the high angle walls meet': the defect sits ON the high-angle jamb line, but the seam that tears is the coplanar one; the mitre widens it ~10x (5273 -> 1444 px without it, 8e5c22727eec) by moving the last vertex of one ladder where the neighbour has none. - `ad0b8faf2405` measurement **greets.displace.old_bake.mitre.profile_double_valued** = "union_profile_double_valued_sides_disagree_up_to_0.08u" (state) {arm=old_bake pose=H6194 scene=greets state=tip55772420} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — THE MITRE WELD IS NOT ONE CURVE. [STONE-FINALV] for the 210 verts on the H6194 corner line (x=17.898, z=-62.952): all 210 are WELD, all ride the same bisector (+0.850,0,+0.527), but the two sheets sample the height map in different UV columns (far wall u=-4.0774, reveal u=+0.5000), their verts are interleaved 1-4 mm apart in y, and MeshOps.cpp builds the shared profile as the UNION of both sides' samples (--greets_displace_profile_agree defaults to 0, so no collapse) - the profile is double-valued and each sheet effectively takes its own displacement: y=1.745 dsp -0.1296 vs -0.1973; y=1.808/1.811 -0.0406 vs -0.0904; and over the whole course y=1.9..3.2 the far wall reads +0.031 (stone, hEff 0.6) while the reveal reads -0.032..-0.055 (its column classed E, hEff 0.321) - the two welded polylines sit ~0.08 u apart for 1.3 u of height. The weld's watertight-by-construction argument (both polylines are re-samplings of ONE piecewise-linear curve) does not hold with a double-valued profile. CAVEAT, measured: inside the H6194 frame (world y ~3.5..6.4 visible) the polyline disagreement is at most 0.037 u (y=3.27: -0.138 vs -0.101; y=4.682/4.683: +0.009 vs -0.027) while the hole there is ~0.09 u wide, so the polyline mismatch alone does NOT account for the in-frame wedge; which sheet is missing there is open (subagent:attack asked to decode the z16 lips).

By crease LENGTH, 73.3% is phase-shifted (best-lag correlation >= 0.8, mean |lag| 1.39 texels - the same relief out of phase, which a dominant-owner rule genuinely reconciles), 5.5% partly related, and 21.2% (1560 u) is TWO UNRELATED CHARTS (best-lag correlation < 0.4) that no blend can reconcile. Sizing a SMOOTH seam by it (cot 5 deg = 11.4) replaced the curved wall with its tangent planes extended a whole unit, each strip standing proud of the curve by the sagitta and the pair mutually satisfying each other mitre trim. A crease is a corner and needs max(cot,tan)(phi/2); a smooth seam is one surface and needs only the mitre tan(phi/2). Max margin 1.059 -> 0.578 u.

Adjacency by COLLINEAR OVERLAP - two edges of different faces on one line (both endpoints within 2e-3 u of the other's line) overlapping over > 5e-3 u, each face's UV interpolated along its own edge at the overlap's ends - sees them: 290 seams against 84 shared edges, the jamb closes to 0.006 tile and the far wall moves as one patch. So the UV discontinuity he sees is EXACTLY the handedness-clone boundary that GreetsFixBitangentHandedness (DEMO/GREETS.CPP:1524, --greets_tbn_fix) draws: it re-points every negative-UV-determinant face onto '<mat>::mirUV' and changes no UV, so the step is the AUTHORED projection phase between a mirrored sheet and its non-mirrored neighbour, ~0.4 tile in u at both places. Consequence for the bake: only these seams sample DIFFERENT height columns on their two sides (the double-valued profile ad0b8faf2405 is specific to them: mitre lines 8-9 and any other line crossing a handedness boundary); every same-family crease already samples the same column on both sides.

At H6194 every dominant hole-bounding face pair shares (or all but shares) the vertex the crack hinges on - |c57/fi259.C - c57/fi78.A| = 0.0020 u - so the minimum pairwise distance reads ~0 while the same seam yawns to 0.0620 u one vertex away and leaks 4585 visible pixels. This is why the rev-dispfix reading that the H6194 bake mesh is watertight at 99% of hole pixels (tri-tri distance 0) coexists with a hole you can see across the room: the metric measures the hinge, not the seam. The correct predicate is per-authored-edge: do the two incident sheets contribute the SAME boundary polyline (symmetric Hausdorff 0, T-junction count 0)? Anything that rested on the min-distance number needs re-deriving.

- `b3b2139d736c` refutation **greets.displace.v4.p4_corners.dz_exit** = "unreachable_by_this_phase" (state) {arm=v4_p4 detector=refrender_diff metric=dz_p90_vs_reference scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5 — REFUTED, as an exit criterion for P4: 'dz p90 < 0.08 u at H6194 / camA / corner6097' cannot be reached by the junction rings, and the mask it was written for cannot even see them. Two measurements say so. (1) THE MASK. tools/v4_p3_ref.sh scores dz through --flat-deg, the BLOCK INTERIOR - more than 10 deg of plane-normal agreement away from any junction - which is precisely the region P4 leaves alone. One binary, one flag apart (--no-v4_rings is the P3 control, byte-identical to P3): camA FLAT p90 0.3345 -> 0.3470, H6194 0.1541 -> 0.1510, corner6097 0.1591 -> 0.1591. (2) THE BUDGET. The complementary CREASE BAND (refrender_diff --crease-band, the same reference and the same per-faceId plane table, boundaries where two 4-neighbour faceIds' plane normals differ by more than 10 deg, dilated) reads, with the reference-coverage tail excluded: camA p50 0.0569 -> 0.0540, p90 0.2590 -> 0.2598; H6194 p50 0.0357 -> 0.0341, p90 0.2215 -> 0.2169; corner6097 p50 0.0386 -> 0.0394, p90 0.1221 -> 0.1244. At radius 2 px, the crease itself, the p50 moves -1.3% / -0.3% / +0.7%. The ring vertices move p50 0.015859 u in total (a7044792d82f), so the residual is two to four times the phase's whole geometric budget. What the residual IS, on file: the P3 block-interior recession 256d9f38b114 - which the crease band inherits because the ring is placed on the arm's own offset plane, i.e. consistent with an interior that is itself displaced too little. P4 makes the mesh SELF-consistent at the junction; it cannot make it agree with the reference while the interior does not.

- `256d9f38b114` measurement **greets.displace.v4.relief.mechanism** = -0.0799 (world_units) {arm=v4_p3 defect=block_interior_unresolved metric=plateau_emr_p50_grid_plateau scene=greets state=P3} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — WHY the P3 relief recesses, localized to one mechanism rather than to the height rule. The wall is a RUNNING BOND: band k+1 s vertical mortar sits at the middle of band k s blocks. At --v4_cpb=1 the target cell IS the block pitch, so ceil(pitch/target)=1 and a block interior carries NO column line of its own -- the [V4-DISPLACE] census reads 8638 plateau nodes against 20308 groove nodes, and every one of them sits on a mortar pad line. The only nodes inside a band-k block come from the union ROW at a band change, where band k+1 s mortar columns are injected, and the row below has nothing under them; the Delaunay then covers the block with triangles whose three corners are ALL mortar-deep. Measured directly at cam A: a triangle at (153.25,127.5) (150.75,127.5) (126.75,189.75) texels with corner heights h=0.048/0.027/0.233, spanning a 57.5-texel block whose field reads h=0.59. Cross-tabulating the field class against the geometric class, the plateau-by-field / plateau-by-grid cell holds 149571 samples at e-r p50 -0.0799 u. This also settles a design premise: 2c s restricted-quadtree rule (adjacent cells differ by <=1 level) is NOT satisfied by the P2 lattice -- a 2.5-texel mortar cell abuts a 57.5-texel block cell, 4.5 levels apart -- and P2 s level_jump_violations=0 measured the row SPLIT COUNT, not the cell sizes.

- `d6188ba3ae0a` refutation **greets.displace.v4.rings.crossings** = "refuted" (state) {arm=v4_ring_cross_both defect=wall pose=camA scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5 — REFUTED: taking the mortar-crossing border samples from BOTH sides of a crease. The first cut of --v4_ring_grooves collected crossings from both adjacent faces grids; at cam A that turned the wall base course into a fan of long facets, plainly visible at 1:1. Localized by one flag on the same binary: --no-v4_ring_grooves removed it entirely, so it is the crossings and not the ring solve. Mechanism: on a wall-to-floor edge the FLOOR flagstone grid crosses the base line at positions the WALL field (the dominant owner, which supplies the height) has no relief at, so those samples densify the border with vertices carrying a height their own neighbourhood does not justify - 160 extra samples of 1361, and at cpb=1 the wall face has no interior node to hold the plateau against them. Restricting the crossings to the DOMINANT OWNER grid removes the artifact and keeps what they exist for, because a crease carries ONE profile by the same ruling that sets the heights (1c196c8c5cea). The refuted rule is kept as --v4_ring_cross_both, default OFF, so the pair stays one flag apart: cross_samples 1201 vs 1361, stone_faces 61430 vs 61742, 31746 px differing by more than 32 levels of 2073600 (1.53%), 3550 px in the 300x180 wall-base box, whole-frame means 0.05 of a level apart.
- `f1dcb9becb86` measurement **greets.displace.v4.rings.held** = 104 (count) {arm=v4_p4 flag=v4_rings metric=corners_held scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — WHAT P4 CANNOT UNPIN, and who holds it. Of 155 authored stone corners, 51 are solved and 104 are held: 48 because they sit on a use-count-1 ABUTMENT (a T-junction against a stone face the exact stitch cannot see, 418c3bf6c9d7 - the host has no vertex on the line, so moving this side alone opens the base junction bc79e39d), and 56 because the point coincides with a face of the same mesh this bake does not touch - measured by name, siling 50 and teleporter 6, which reproduces P1 own foreign-neighbour census (siling 50, teleporter 6) exactly. Of 1906 shared-border samples 1386 are solved, 246 held by an abutment and 274 held against a non-baked face. So the wall-to-wall creases DO now displace and the wall-to-CEILING boundary does not: moving a stone vertex away from an undisplaced siling face would tear that seam, which is a fact about the bake set, not about the ring solve. --v4_ring_abut is the measured arm that lifts the abutment half.
- `a7044792d82f` measurement **greets.displace.v4.rings.move** = 0.015859 (world_units) {arm=v4_p4 flag=v4_rings metric=ring_vertex_offset_p50 scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5 — HOW FAR THE RING PHASE ACTUALLY MOVES THE JUNCTION, as a distribution rather than a max. Over the 1437 solved ring vertices the offset |x| from the authored position is p50 0.015859 u, p90 0.028126 u, p99 0.164141 u, max 0.205511 u, with 81 of 1437 (5.6%) under 0.005 u. The census had only ever printed max_move=0.205511 and d=[-0.164141..+0.033643], which reads like a fifth of a unit of movement and is in fact one vertex. This number is the ceiling on what P4 can change: the dz-vs-reference residual at the crease band is 0.038-0.057 u p50 at the three exit poses, i.e. two to four times the MEDIAN ring offset, so no placement of these vertices - not even an exact one - could bring that band under the proposal's 0.08 u p90. Read from [V4-RINGS] move on the shipped arm (--greets-displace --greets_displace_v4, rings and grooves at their defaults).
- `efef6e9e0158` measurement **greets.displace.v4.rings.residual** = 2.776e-17 (world_units) {arm=v4_p4 flag=v4_rings metric=ring_offset_plane_residual scene=greets state=P4} w=3/5  [unverified_at_tip] — The 2e ring invariant HOLDS EXACTLY where it can, and CANNOT hold where the design did not look. Every ring vertex is placed on the offset plane of every chart meeting it: over 1021 one-plane, 538 two-plane and 22 exactly-three-plane rings the max residual |n_i.(P-P0) - d| is 2.776e-17 u, against the 1e-5 u 2e asks - the closed forms are exact. But 12 ring vertices have FOUR OR MORE distinct chart planes, where the system is over-determined and has NO exact solution at all; 2e says 3-plane corner solve and does not cover them. Solving three exactly and ignoring the rest read 8.872e-03 u on the ignored planes; the least-squares point (NtN)x = d.Nt1 spreads it to 4.407e-03 u, which is what ships. Also measured: 0 mitre-limit clamps at limit 4, 0 singular (180 deg) folds, 0 ill-conditioned triples, max ring move 0.2055 u with d in [-0.1641, +0.0336].

- **greets.displace.v4.tear** — 6 current records, by arm/detector/flag/pose/state; no single current value
    - `d5b5dbb0f1d4` arm=v4_band_union detector=v4_tear_cover flag=∅ pose=∅ state=P3: 18 (hole_px) — --v4_band_union, the only measured fix for the plateau recession, FAILS the tear cap: 18 hole px over 9 of 54 poses against a 15 px cap. The 4-px difference is not silhouette recession, which is the one thing a displaced arm may legitimately move -- every new pixel is an INTERIOR gap with all 8 neighbours rasterised in BOTH arms and all 8 stone in the control, and they are COLLINEAR, the signature of a crack along one line: G2 (1457,49) (1378,70) (1299,91) spacing (-79,+21), P5813 (1280,488) (1293,668) on floor::mirUV. Decomposed on those poses (P2 lattice amp0 / band-union amp0 / band-union amp0.3 / no-union amp0.3): G2 0/0/3/0, P5813 2/2/3/1, P5854 0/0/1/1 -- so the G2 and P5813 pinholes are the band-union x displacement INTERACTION, neither the lattice change alone nor the displacement alone produces one. Mechanism consistent with the union placing two column lines from DIFFERENT bands within a texel of each other on one row, where the relief class flips between them and the surface takes a 0.16 u step across ~1 texel; kLineMergeTex is 1.0 texel and does not see the class. This is what reverted the default in f53dc4d6.

It is arithmetically a no-op for the texel FETCH (the swizzled address masks with (1<<LogWidth)-1 and UScaleFactor is 1<<LogWidth), and it is NOT a no-op for the rendered frame -- measured on four independent arms (c2d4e18d5a44). Consequence: an arm that unifies UV PHASE across planes changes the render even where its own census says the visible (mod-1) shift is 8.7e-5 tile, and that difference will read as 77-99% of pixels differing at mean 15-44 of 765 on a snapshot diff. Do not read such a diff as 'the pattern moved' without a row-profile correlation: at camA the wall crop correlates at 0.9967 with a best shift of -1 px, i.e. the block courses did not move at all.

Harness first validated -- a rewrite that writes the authored UVs back byte-for-byte reproduces the control md5 exactly (2f61d079e4583cf323161309a261009c, --v4_flat arm at camA t=5965), so the rewrite path itself moves nothing. Then a PURE +1.0 tile translation of every stone U and V (a throwaway patch, `nu = *pu[k] + 1.0`) changes the render at BOTH texture-filter settings, with mips disabled, and in the forward path: tf=2 f89287568b8d3ed4ef5f83b25abec3ed vs control 2f61d079..., tf=0 bb5ccbd7cdb4c15a5d54e9c610ce563a vs control 55e38cae37155bb25f30b7f704184f58, --no-mips afe4e93afc952b98cfd2afb54ff3c0eb vs eddf4c9d156b9b5b020e355df2f3252b, forward 47b8fc1eb7552d0f11be703b977f9c2d vs 90d80190d8eb4e9596416823db73a06a. It is not uvToVertices (byte-identical with that call removed), not mip selection (survives --no-mips), not the bilinear negative-UV path (survives tf=0), and not the swizzled address mask (UScaleFactor = 1<<LogWidth and the mask is (1<<LogWidth)-1, so the FETCH is periodic in one tile by construction). Remaining suspect, untested: the per-face TANGENT basis, which is recomputed from float differences of the shifted UVs (MeshOps.cpp faceTangent, PREPROC Compute_Vertex_Tangents) and feeds normal-mapped shading everywhere. Value = the number of arms in which the +1.0 translation was measured to change the render, of 4 tested.

- `ba7c2e825b1f` trap **greets.render.mip_is_per_polygon** = 265108 (count) {granularity=whole_frame metric=miplevel scene=greets} w=3/5  [floating] — THE ALBEDO MIP IS A FUNCTION OF THE TESSELLATION, NOT ONLY OF THE CAMERA. FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP:945 picks the miplevel per CLIPPED POLYGON: under MinSize = XRes*YRes*0.02 pixels (41472 at 1920x1080) one mip is taken for the whole polygon from 0.5*log2(texArea*SizeX*SizeY/pixArea); above it the mipmap-via-subdivision path splits the polygon and each piece gets its own. So subdividing a wall changes which mip its pixels sample even when every new vertex lies exactly on the authored plane: 265108 pixels change mip (0->1 on 225884, 0->2 on 39224) between the 226 authored stone triangles and a 60606-triangle UNDISPLACED lattice of the same planes at greets cam A, and the SHIPPED bake at amplitude 0 shows the same signature. Consequence for any 'byte-identical after retessellation' plan: it cannot hold. Consequence for any A/B whose arms differ in face count: the colour delta is not attributable to the shading change under test until the mip plane is differenced -- the G-buffer packs it in bits 28..31 of the mat32 word, dumpable with FDS_SNAPSHOT_GBUFDUMP and read by tools/v4_p2_planes.py.

- `5ffcbd4ab8a7` refutation **greets.ssao.gtao.bias_form** = 0.166 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024 flag=ssao_gtao_bias metric=near_wall_ao_std_under_the_angle_bias_form pose=t5836 scene=greets} w=5/5 — THE HBAO-STYLE ANGLE BIAS IS THE WRONG FORM HERE - BUILT, MEASURED, REVERTED. The t=5726 trace made it look obviously right: the two self-samples sat at |d| 0.066 and 0.299 and BOTH at dot(d,N)/|d| = 0.070, a constant 4.0 deg tilt (the bump-perturbed G-buffer normal disagreeing with the real surface), which a scale-free angle threshold handles without eating far-field occlusion. Implemented as sin(elev) = dot(d,N)*dinv and measured: it does NOT fix the t=5836 grazing near-wall banding at all - std 0.166 at 0.05 and 0.163 at 0.10 against 0.146 unfixed, versus 0.073 for the plane-DISTANCE form. That wall's error is the ZPage16 depth quantum, a fixed SIZE, not a fixed angle. Generalising from one traced pixel to the whole defect class was the mistake. The distance form is what the flag ships; the angle form is recorded here and in the flag help so it is not re-derived from the same trace.

- `c1c3a8672b39` refutation **greets.ssao.gtao.edge_band.mitigation** = 16.8 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_edge_clamp metric=pct_increase_in_mean_abs_border_error pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — CLAMP-TO-EDGE SAMPLING DOES NOT FIX THE GTAO BORDER BAND HERE - IT OVERSHOOTS. --ssao_gtao_edge_clamp replaces the off-frame `continue` (open horizon) with a clamp of the sample coordinate into the frame. Measured against the synthetic border (--ssao_edge_inset=256, where the un-truncated answer exists): inside 256 px of that border the un-mitigated error is +0.0860 AO (too bright) and with edge_clamp it is -0.0361 (too DARK); mean |error| rises 0.0860 -> 0.1004, i.e. the mitigation makes it 16.8 % WORSE in absolute terms. Mechanism: a whole run of march steps collapses onto one border pixel, which reads as a solid occluder at a fixed angle. It is at least strictly localised - the interior delta is exactly 0.0000 - so it costs nothing outside the band. The other two arms priced at the same pose: --ssao_gtao_edge_fade=256 lifts the band +0.1032 (it simply DISCARDS the AO there, interior 0.0000); --ssao_gtao_srad_max=1024 moves the INTERIOR too (+0.0288) because the same 4 steps then span 1024 px and lose near-contact occlusion (meanAO 0.7796 -> 0.8111).
- `a0fefb201622` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.resolution_dependence** = 12.6 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao metric=pp_swing_in_ao_lt_0p9_over_1280_to_2560 pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — GTAO AO IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE SCENE - it moves with the pixel grid, and the hemisphere sampler does not. Same camera, same geometry, same flags, only --force_xres/--force_yres changed: GTAO ao<0.9 = 73.7 % at 1280x720, 66.5 % at 1920x1080, 61.1 % at 2560x1440 (meanAO 0.7732 / 0.7795 / 0.7928) - a 12.6-POINT swing from resolution alone. The --no-ssao_gtao hemisphere control at the same two of those resolutions gives 44.4 % and 44.3 % (meanAO 0.8778 / 0.8776), i.e. FLAT. Mechanism: the 256-px cap binds, so the world AO radius is cap*viewZ/FOVX, and FOVX is proportional to XRes - the radius therefore scales with depth AND inversely with horizontal resolution. This is the view-dependent shading Gil-Ad reported, and it is why his ladder cleared --no-ssao_gtao.

- `9f6575e68c03` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.round_truncation** = 0.0044 (pct_delta) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_round_fix metric=rms_ao_delta_of_the_fix pose=t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — REAL BUG, TOP/LEFT-SIGNED, SECOND ORDER. The GTAO march computed its sample pixel as `px + int(off + 0.5f)`, and C truncation toward zero makes that round-half-up ONLY for off >= -0.5; every negative offset lands ONE PIXEL SHORT. It does not average out over the rotations, because buildSliceTrig builds phi = (s + jit)*(PI/slices) with jit in [0,1], so phi never leaves [0,PI) and sin(phi) - the y component of every slice direction at all 16 rotations - is non-negative: every UP-going sample landed 1 px low and every LEFT-going sample 1 px right, while down/right were exact. Fixed by rounding to nearest (--ssao_gtao_round_fix, default ON, one instruction CHEAPER). Delta at cam A: rms 0.0044 AO, max 0.0908, 3.39 % of px move >0.01; asymmetric in the predicted direction - top 256-px band mean -0.00185 (6.82 % of px >0.01) vs bottom +0.00186 (1.12 %), left -0.00140 (4.24 %) vs right -0.00068 (0.03 %).

From the --ssao_dump view-Z plane at 1920x1080 (FOVX=1728, --ssao_radius=4, the zfloor inert in greets) the unclamped srad median is 894 px at t=5965, 1034 at t=5743 and 9333 at t=6097, and the cap binds on 99.99 / 92.12 / 100.00 % of covered pixels.
