- **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.frame_ms** — 2 current records, by arm/flag/metric; no single current value  (+1 superseded)
    - `846db4d1125f` arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace flag=ssao_gtao_step_dist metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta: 0.12 (pct_delta) [unverified_at_tip] — COST-NEUTRAL, and the SSAO wave itself is slightly CHEAPER. greets t=5743, 1920x1080, --deferred_prof=5 --bench iters=40, 11 INTERLEAVED runs per arm on ONE binary, wall_min: renderFrame 49.792 (uniform/256) vs 49.853 (geometric/1024), +0.061 ms = +0.12 %, inside the +/-1.5-2 % placement floor; the ssao wave 7.140 -> 6.853 (-4.02 %) and ssao-march 5.007 -> 4.728 (-5.57 %), with the medians agreeing in sign (renderFrame median is LOWER for the geometric arm, 50.336 vs 51.482). Mechanism for the march being cheaper rather than dearer: the step count is unchanged, but a ~894-px march puts more samples OFF-FRAME, and the off-frame path skips the depth gather, both gtaoAcos calls and the bitmask build - and a whole 8-lane batch with no live sample takes the `if (!any) continue` exit. The per-cell ladder constants cost two quadratic minimax evaluations, not libm.
    - `aa1c1a3982bc` arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-stepdist1-srad1024-guards-bias02-displace flag=ssao_gtao_bias metric=renderFrame_wall_min_pct_delta_vs_preround_default: 0.27 (pct_delta) — 11 INTERLEAVED --bench pairs on ONE binary (arms differ only by flags: the OLD arm is --ssao_gtao_step_dist=0 --ssao_gtao_srad_max=256 --no-ssao_gtao_round_fix, proved byte-exact against the pre-round build), iters=40, 1920x1080 forced, --deferred_prof=5 --profiler=0, dummy drivers, wall_min. THE SHIPPED FLIP'S renderFrame COST AT GREETS IS NOT RESOLVABLE ABOVE THE PLACEMENT FLOOR, and the +1.40 % this supersedes is not reproduced. renderFrame 46.240 -> 46.367 ms (+0.127 ms, +0.27 %) against a +/-1.5-2 % floor; a SECOND independent 11-round battery on the same binary minutes earlier gave 46.183 -> 45.888 (-0.64 %), i.e. the two batteries straddle zero, which is what 'inside the floor' means. The ROW numbers are stable and DO clear their floor: ssao 6.914 -> 7.169 (+3.69 %), ssao-march 4.838 -> 5.087 (+5.15 %), ssao-apply +0.06 %, ssao-blur +1.15 % - and those reproduce the superseded record's own row figures (+3.64 % / +5.47 %) to within a tenth of a point. So the guards' per-row cost is confirmed; what is corrected is the claim that it shows up as +1.4 % of the frame. Mechanism unchanged: the SIMD march MASKS rejected samples rather than branching around them, so a rejected sample costs its full arithmetic plus the guard, and the guards give back the off-frame early-out that made the unguarded geometric arm look free.
- `e279eac2ea39` measurement **greets.ssao.gtao.step_dist.near_step_artifacts** = 0.03 (share_pct) {arm=deferred-hdr-hdrlinear-tf2-ssao-gtao-displace-stepdist1-srad1024 flag=ssao_gtao_thick_clamp metric=pct_fully_black_px_in_the_saw_tooth_column pose=t5726 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — BOTH OF HIS ARTIFACTS ARE ONE ROOT CAUSE, AND IT IS NOT A DEPTH-DISCONTINUITY BUG. Traced at t=5726 px(1129,225): the black saw-tooth column is NOT the near wall occluding the far background - those samples are correctly rejected (OUT-OF-RANGE, |d| = 31.5 against a 4 u radius). The black pixels are ON THE NEAR WALL, grazing, just inside its own silhouette, occluding THEMSELVES. Two guards were missing because the uniform ladder put the first sample 48-192 px out and never needed them. (a) THICKNESS: the bitmask back face is d - V*thickness, a flat world constant, so when |d| is much smaller than thickness it lands BEHIND the receiver and acos(dot(b,V)) saturates at PI - in the trace one sample at |d| = 0.066 set 11 of 32 sectors and one at 0.285 set THIRTY, and the saturation vanishes at the first step whose |d| (1.274) exceeds thickness (1.0). Fixed by --ssao_gtao_thick_clamp: back face bounded by min(thickness,|d|). (b) SELF-OCCLUSION: --ssao_bias is read ONLY by the hemisphere sampler, so GTAO shipped with no bias at all; at ~5 px the ZPage16 quantum (0.0025 u) and the half-res cell offset become a horizon at grazing incidence. Fixed by --ssao_gtao_bias (plane distance, AUTO 0.05 u). RESULTS at 1512x848 on his exact arm: t=5726 column fully-black fraction 32.90 % -> 0.03 % (std 0.343 -> 0.216); t=5836 column 8.23 % -> 0.00 % (std 0.280 -> 0.148, the shipped uniform arm is 0.147); t=5836 near-wall banding std 0.1462 -> 0.0725, BELOW the shipped uniform arm's 0.0796. Isolation: bias alone leaves 11.58 %/1.36 % black (the clamp owns the columns), clamp alone leaves the banding at std 0.139 (the bias owns the banding). --ssao_downscale=1 changes neither (37.59 %/9.86 % black), so the bilateral upsample is NOT implicated - that hypothesis is refuted.

- **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.causal_ab** — 2 current records, by (no distinguishing scope); no single current value  (+1 superseded)
    - `d585fec1a494` : "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) [unverified_at_tip] — THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. THIS RECORD'S VALUE IS THE UNGUARDED REPRO SNAPSHOT md5, 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, whose ceiling band (rows 5..40, cols 600..1300) reads 82.8/63.4/47.6 against the M5's measured 85.3/65.8/47.8. The other three arms of the same battery: shipping d92cb6f5eb19da4301588ae83af6a56e = 85.2/133.5/139.0; idshift + --shadow_coplanar_guard 8dbbafb77629598e5a3e2b1bfaae9e1a = 85.1/133.8/139.3, i.e. with EVERY identity rescue in the scene destroyed the guard alone carries the frame back to within 0.3/255 of the shipping look; idshift + guard + --shadow_coplanar_guard_onesided bc7a68cc2e5eeec1ca4ab8ef350b5658, which is --no-shadow_polyid BYTE FOR BYTE (so requiring the depth test to agree degenerates to depth mode and the SYMMETRIC band is the actual guard). The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies.
    - `efbf02b4143e` : "83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281" (snapshot_md5) [unverified_at_tip] — recheck of d585fec1a494: THE M5 CEILING REPRODUCED ON A CORRECT MACHINE, AND THE GUARD RESTORES IT. greets cam A t=5965, 1920x1080, dev box, clean Release at 81c4db52. --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 stamps id+N into the shadow map's id half leaving the DEPTH half untouched, so every receiver's own texels carry a non-matching id at ZERO depth separation - the M5's ceiling state, frame-wide. The raster-noise lever --shadow_coplanar_flip=K (last writer wins within K quanta) is much weaker: K=1 moves 3 px, K<=8 moves at most 406 px in one 250x160 box and NEVER the ceiling band - exact and near-exact ownership ties are rare in these maps, so tie-flipping alone cannot be the whole M5 story; the M5 needs the id half itself to differ, which its pervasive map divergence supplies. — 'cd Runtime && rm -rf /tmp/sgrc && SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy FDS_GREETS_CAM="22.5084476,3.87992334,-61.8882256,-0.829246342,-0.20816116,0.518670499" ./DEMO --deferred --hdr --hdr-linear --texture-filter=2 --ssao --ssao-gtao --greets-displace --force_xres=1920 --force_yres=1080 --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 --snapshot=greets@t=5965 --out=/tmp/sgrc >/dev/null 2>&1 && md5 -q /tmp/sgrc/greets_t005965_color.ppm' -> 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281 (was 83d7065a654b5cb45cab7d00a9b94281, agrees)
- `79bedaa08607` refutation **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.constant_quanta** = 512 (count) {arm=idshift_guard_constN metric=guard_band_quanta pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5 — REFUTED: a SMALL CONSTANT tolerance cannot be the co-planar guard's band. The brief proposed N = 1-2 shadow-map depth quanta. Measured at greets cam A t=5965 under the --shadow_coplanar_idshift=32768 repro, the ceiling band stays at the fully-shadowed 82.8/63.4/47.6 for N = 2, 8, 64 AND 512, and only at N = 4096 does it start to recover (83.6/103.4/104.6). VALUE = 512, the largest constant band measured that rescues NOTHING. MECHANISM: the deltas are SLOPE-sized, not quantum-sized - the receiver's depth is sampled at the PIXEL's exact position while the stored depth is the occluder's depth at the TEXEL it won, so on a slanted surface (texel footprint x depth slope) is thousands of quanta even when the two polygons are exactly co-planar. This is the same quantity --shadow_slope_bias (1024, reaching 4096 at the 0.2 N.L clamp) exists to cover in depth mode. The guard's default band is therefore --shadow_coplanar_guard_quanta = -1 = the DEPTH-MODE BIAS for that very pixel (shadow_bias + the slope-scaled term), which is narrow (512) where the surface faces the light and wide (up to 4608) where it grazes. THE TRADE a larger N buys: a real occluder standing within N quanta of the receiver loses its last N quanta of contact shadow.
- `ad6b638504d1` measurement **lever.shadow_coplanar_guard.on_arm_cost** = 19.92 (share_pct) {arm=guard_on metric=px_changed_vs_off pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets} w=5/5  [unverified_at_tip] — WHAT THE FLAG-ON ARM COSTS ON THE DEV BOX, MEASURED not assumed. tools/render_gate.sh under FDS_SHADOW_COPLANAR_GUARD=1 is still 4/4 PASS with all four rows BYTE-IDENTICAL - those scenes have no co-planar shadow-group conflict. greets does, and it moves a lot. Same recipe, OFF -> ON: cam A t=5965 413,078 px differ (VALUE = 19.92%% of frame), 124,786 px >8, max delta 136, signed mean +4.63, 412,590 brighter vs 477 darker; default-camera t=1588 789,885 px (38.09%%), 155,842 >8, max 120, mean +3.69; t=2845 558,382 px (26.93%%), 292,069 >8, max 140, mean +12.07; t=5965 304,548 px (14.69%%), 99,823 >8, max 138, mean +4.75. DIRECTION: >=99.6%% of every changed pixel is BRIGHTER - the guard removes shadow and never adds any, which is what it is built to do, but on greets it removes a great deal. Whether that is wrongly-cast self-shadow (correct) or real contact shadow (a look regression) is HIS eye's call, not these counters'. PERF, single run --deferred_prof=1 at cam A: DeferredLighting-call 22.133 -> 24.697 ms (+2.56 ms, +11.6%%), lighting-w1 17.503 -> 20.002 ms (+2.50 ms), lighting-w2 2.549 -> 2.645 ms; most of it is the disarmed --deferred_cube_prepass, whose cached tail is called with lz=0 and so cannot serve the guard. Default stays OFF.

### measure
- `015ae8c8e985` trap **measure.concurrent_merge_contaminated_arm** = "trap" (state) {state=rwdi_test_2026_08_30 tool=m5} w=1/5  [floating] — The dev-box RelWithDebInfo ceiling test was CONTAMINATED by my own concurrent merge: the GTAO-defaults merge landed on fog-wt between the Release reference render and the RWDI build, so the RWDI arm silently carried the NEW AO defaults and the 96%-of-pixels difference measured the flag flip, not -O2. The only valid comparison from that batch remains M5 vs the pre-merge local Release (24.79%, the ceiling block). Rule: an A/B across builds must pin the SOURCE SHA of both arms (detached worktree at a fixed commit), never build from a branch another task is advancing. The provenance sidecars carry the SHA - read them before diffing.

### perf
- `8f04128dc038` law **perf.floor** = 2.0 (pct_delta) {granularity=kernel_row metric=delta_pct} w=5/5  [floating] — a single kernel row's between-binary delta carries a +-1.5-2 % code-placement floor: 24 bytes of inert padding moved greets lighting-w1 by 1.21 %, and an arm with the parent's exact 5359-instruction kernel still carried +1.08 % (00w).
- `d5f079501e3c` law **perf.law.L1_fanout_threshold** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — A fan-out pays only above a work-per-dispatch threshold of ~tens of microseconds; under ~100 us the round trip is the cost (city glass -24 %, RTT cone pass +139 %, mirror-mask clear +33 %). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `95a401b2efdd` law **perf.law.L2_scalar_ilp** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — An 8-iteration independent scalar loop is not automatically improved by vectorising it: the scalar form already extracts ILP; price it in cycles, never in instructions (SSAO gather -9.8 % instr, +2.5 % cycles). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `2a4700fe068d` law **perf.law.L3_register_pressure** = "holds" (state) {granularity=kernel_row} w=5/5  [floating] — Register pressure beats op count in the cone and lighting kernels: C6 removed 10 vector-ALU ops and paid 29 spills, +5.42 % cycles; one __m256 is two of arm64's 32 v-registers. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `35721b7adc41` law **perf.law.L4_issue_bound** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — Once a kernel is issue-bound instruction counts stop predicting time; the cone pass at ~81 % of NEON issue converts instructions to wall, lighting-w1 does not (-2.0 % instr -> +0.7..1.4 % cycles). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `3c27392e58f7` law **perf.law.L5_cores_triage** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating] — cores = Gcyc / clock / wall finds both the wins and the refutations: a row far below the worker count is serial or barrier-bound, one at the worker count is compute-bound. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `0236fb850a7c` law **perf.law.L6_fewer_pairs** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — The only lever with real headroom on a saturated per-pixel row is FEWER PAIRS: micro-optimising the body is exhausted (cone pass -52.6 % by range cull; greets lighting-w1 8.40 lights/px enter, 2.06-3.40 accumulate). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `e55e19b2c7a2` law **perf.law.L7_share_source** = "holds" (state) {} w=5/5  [floating] — Bit-exactness is won by SHARING SOURCE, not transcribing: the froxel reflection leg went 8-wide bit-exact first build by lifting the leg into one static inline helper both paths call. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `4a4c2a5695d3` law **perf.law.L8_per_tile_decidable** = "holds" (state) {} w=1/5  [floating] — Anything that runs per-pixel and is decidable per-tile or per-light is the first place to look, and the guard usually already exists - check before proposing it. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `6761cfcf6bae` law **perf.law.L9_runtime_predicate** = "holds" (state) {row=lighting-w1 scene=greets} w=5/5  [floating] — In a kernel at its register-allocation limit a new runtime predicate costs more than the work it removes: a never-taken skip inside the cube tap cost +12.4 %, a runtime bool hatch +4.3 % with the flag OFF; the cube tap only gets cheaper by being CALLED less. - `58dd332647a8` trap **perf.trap.T3_hwread_process_wide** = "trap" (state) {tool=TailProf} w=1/5  [floating] — TailProf::hwRead() is proc_pid_rusage(getpid()) - PROCESS-WIDE; a nested scope inside a threaded pass measures every thread. Split threaded sub-scopes by ablation differencing, never by nesting a timer. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `d5d5ac74d34d` trap **perf.trap.T4_chdir_asset_root** = "trap" (state) {tool=DEMO} w=1/5  [floating] — ChdirToAssetRoot makes a binary's FILE LOCATION select its assets: DEMO chdirs to its own directory, so a worktree binary always renders that worktree's assets. Three false alarms traced to this; gate with --no-chdir_assets. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `76b2bfb5c3e4` trap **perf.trap.T5_chase_pose_sequence** = "trap" (state) {scene=chase tool=snapshot} w=1/5  [floating] — chase pins are POSE-SEQUENCE dependent: t=800 alone differs from t=800 third of five by 434 591 px (20.96 %, max 5); only the first pose of a process is comparable across recipes. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `69fbcb8b6628` trap **perf.trap.T6_pgrep_self_match** = "trap" (state) {tool=pgrep} w=1/5  [floating] — pgrep -f matches your own shell: two agents' box-quiet loops deadlocked on each other's pgrep command lines. Use ps -Ao comm= | grep -c '/DEMO$'. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from docs/PERF_LAWS.md]
- `e088f40341b3` trap **perf.trap.T7_ssao_dump_inflates** = "trap" (state) {flag=ssao_dump} w=1/5  [floating] — --ssao_dump forces the scalar apply loop and inflates the pass it measures ~3.5x (4.2 -> 13.7-16.0 ms); a correctness instrument, never a perf one. - `e3623046e07e` trap **perf.trap.T8_cmake_install_rpath** = "trap" (state) {tool=cmake} w=1/5  [floating] — cmake --install fails on a pre-existing rpath step (install_name_tool: no LC_RPATH) - that is NOT a failed build; the binary is placed by the POST_BUILD copy. - `10e91a31d5d8` measurement **platform.m5.divergence_mechanism** = "fpcr_ah_set_by_fpu_lprecision" (state) {tool=m5} w=4/5  [unverified_at_tip] — ROOT CAUSE of the M5 divergence, same binary and assets on both machines: FDS/Base/FDS_VARS.H FPU_LPrecision() wrote FPCR = FZ|AH (0x1000002) on every thread that calls it (REV.CPP, MainLoop, Snapshot, SDL2, and every render worker via GENERAL.H). AH (bit 1) is ARMv8.7 FEAT_AFP 'alternate handling'. The M2 Max lacks FEAT_AFP: the bit is RES0, the write is silently dropped, workers read back 0x1000000 (AH=0) - plain FZ, denormal inputs AND outputs flushed - and that is the mode every pin was measured under. The M5 Max implements FEAT_AFP and honours the bit: workers read 0x1000002 (AH=1), which changes (a) FNEG/FABS - a NaN's sign bit is left unchanged, (b) FZ flushes outputs only, denormal inputs pass, (c) FRECPE/FRSQRTE precision (FEAT_RPRES), (d) FMIN/FMAX NaN propagation. Probe (scratchpad fpprobe/ahprobe.c): on the M5 AH=1 -> fneg(0x7FFFFFFF7FFFFFFF) UNCHANGED, AH=0 -> FLIPPED; the M2 cannot set the bit; fresh processes and threads are FPCR=0 on both, so the demo's own write is the only source. (a) alone is the transparent-panel / spire-orb defect: clang emits g_rasterXExtent={INT32_MAX,-1} as mvni.2s+fneg+str (a NaN bit pattern negated), so under AH=1 the extent's hi stays INT32_MAX and every xpar composite range is empty (raster_extent_bound_px, composite_filled_px). FIX: FPU_LPrecision now writes base|FZ (AH and FIZ cleared), reason in the comment. RESULT with the fixed binary (dev build md5 fe514908..., shipped to the M5 as Runtime/DEMO_dev, install_name_tool to /opt/homebrew/opt/sdl2 + ad-hoc codesign): greets t=1043 M5 cbe52ab0... -> f96caaf922e291dab771f8dfbfc485c2 = dev; fountain t=500 M5 048f3905... -> 4d62d5818c8b030061081e6c828e5547 = dev; fountain sweep t=100/300/500/800/1200 identical 5/5 (1d6c31fe/ba375e66/f8a20833/0b0fe2f9/d7efd097); tools/render_gate.sh on the M5 4/4 at the dev hashes (4ac809e5/826c09e6/b41894f9/166fa25a, where the M5 used to read 4043aaaa/88dd4632/ffa28566/1a98cdf6); tools/ovec/gates.sh on the M5 11 of 12 rows equal to the dev pins (greets t=1588, the four greets-acc poses 08f0741f/84e18552/0cc05784/456eac7d, fountain 0222f903, chase x7, three city-acc arms). REMAINING: the plain city row (FDS_CITY_ENV_PIXEL=1 city@t=1961 --deferred) reads 0debc80a on the M5 vs bd4ffbf8 on dev - deterministic on both, unchanged by an empty cache dir and by FDS_THREADS=1 on both, 3.358% of pixels differ, 0.004% by more than 8, max 18, bbox x[0,1687] y[0,641] - a small last-ulp-class residual, filed separately (platform.m5.city_residual). Dev is byte-null under the fix: render_gate 4/4, ovec 12/12, greets t=1043 and fountain t=500 unchanged.

- `dad4de8f8962` measurement **platform.m5.polyid_has_no_depth_term** = "identity_only_no_bias" (state) {defect=ceiling_colour scene=greets state=mechanism_2026_08_31 tool=m5} w=1/5  [unverified_at_tip] — MECHANISM CONFIRMED FROM THE CODE (docs/SHADOW_GUARD.md §1): ShadowMode::PolyId decides occlusion by IDENTITY ALONE - `occ += w iff c != 0 && c != receiverId` in CubeShadow_Tail (FDS/FILLERS/ShadowMap.h), with NO depth term and NO bias; DeferredShadowSampling.h passes constBias=0/slopeBias=0 into it on purpose. Depth mode's constBias(512)+slopeBias(<=4096) IS the acne rescue and it is OWNERSHIP-BLIND, so a co-planar occluder reads LIT whoever wrote the texel - which is exactly why --no-shadow_polyid restores the M5 ceiling while the default shadows it. PolyId's only rescue is id equality, so a different-shadow-group co-planar neighbour hard-shadows the receiver with ZERO depth separation from its own plane. Ownership is decided by `enc > z_existing` in ShadowBarry, i.e. by ONE quantum, and the M5's maps diverge on 41/48 static maps. SHARPENING of the parked hypothesis: the id is a MATERIAL/shadow-group id (Material::ShadowMatID, else ID+1), NOT a per-polygon id, so same-group co-planar polygons never fight - and greets allocates 2183 per-plane-cluster shadow groups by design ([PIRAMID-SPLIT] census), which is 2183 chances for the mismatch. The predicate is copied in FOUR places: the cube tail, LightmapBake.cpp SampleStaticCubeAtWorld (the path greets STATIC omnis actually take), the spot tap in DeferredSurfaceKernel.cpp, and the cached tail in CubeTapPrepass.h.

Follow-up question filed with the discriminating commands.  (+2 superseded)

That is the same order as the +-1-quantum speckle both machines show (10,376 texels bare / 10,493 displaced), and a +-1-ULP fuzz of the clipper inputs reproduces 476-1033 such texels locally.

Use pixel_aspect_x = 1, pixel_aspect_y = 1.3333333 (ref.py --pax 1 --pay 1.3333333); the vertical-scale sweep then peaks at f = 1.00 (0.618 vs the FDS reference) and is below 0.19 one step either side. Second trap in the same tool: argparse reads a --cam value with a leading minus as an option, so the corner pose (-9.2,...) must be passed as --cam=...; in a batch it failed silently and the frame was simply missing. - `ccd53bb80c90` trap **tool.cmake.language_cxx_on_mm** = "trap" (state) {tool=cmake} w=1/5  [floating] — set_source_files_properties(... LANGUAGE CXX) on Objective-C++ .mm sources emits an explicit -x c++ since CMake CMP0119 (>=3.20; NEW by default with a fresh 4.x cache), overriding the .mm extension: clang warns argument unused for -ObjC++ and then rejects every @class in the Foundation headers. Bit the M5 (fresh CLT + fresh cache) while the dev box coasted on an old cache. Fix: enable_language(OBJCXX) in the gated subdirectory and drop the LANGUAGE override; -fobjc-arc stays as a per-file option. Verified: GpuBench configures and links on the dev box with the fix.

Compare against a headless CPU frame without the overlay instead.  (+1 superseded)
- `1c446f7c1625` trap **tool.groundwork.amend_orphans_head** = "trap" (state) {tool=groundwork} w=1/5  [floating] — Records auto-capture the repo HEAD at write time. Writing records and then AMENDING (or rebasing away) that commit leaves their head pointing at a commit that is no longer an ancestor of the tip, so the tool treats them as unmerged-branch records: they can neither re-verify anything nor count as at-tip. Happened 2026-08-30: four agreeing render_gate rows written at f02e31b4, commit amended to 5381c68b, the four baselines stayed unverified (36 instead of 32). Rule: write records, then make a NEW commit; never amend/rebase a commit that records were written against without re-filing them.

### trap
- `609d12df45a9` trap **trap.miplevel_minsize_frozen_static** = 5242.88 (count) {metric=mip_minsize pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5  [floating] — MiplevelClipper (FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP) computes its small-face threshold as `static const float MinSize = XRes*YRes * 0.02;` - a FUNCTION-LOCAL static, initialised ONCE by whichever thread calls first, from whatever the XRes/YRes globals held then. MEASURED at greets cam A on a 1920x1080 run: MinSize = 5242.88, i.e. 512x512 - an offscreen bake got there first, so the MAIN view runs the whole process with a threshold 7.9x too small (it should be 41472). The source comment ("update if XRes and/or YRes changes...") documents the bug and nothing does it. Consequences: the poly-split path is entered for faces meant to take the uniform-mip fast path, the value silently depends on WHICH pass touches the clipper first (a scheduling property), and a window resize never updates it. NOT the cause of the M5 divergence - both branches still rasterise the face, verified - but a live resolution-dependence trap for anyone reading mip behaviour or pricing the split path.

### zsh

## From the global store
- `5158416a130a` trap **aarch64.fpcr_ah.fneg_nan_int_pair** = "trap" (exact) {project=revival-fog tool=aarch64} w=2/5  [floating]  ‹global› — Never set FPCR.AH (bit 1, ARMv8.7 FEAT_AFP 'alternate handling') unless every semantic it changes is wanted: with AH=1 FNEG/FABS leave a NaN's sign bit unchanged, FZ flushes outputs only (FIZ governs inputs), FRECPE/FRSQRTE precision changes (FEAT_RPRES), FMIN/FMAX NaN propagation changes. Apple M1/M2 lack FEAT_AFP - the bit is RES0 and the write is silently dropped - so code that sets it looks correct for years and then diverges on the first FEAT_AFP core (Apple M5). The specific bite: clang materialises a 64-bit integer pair whose lanes are 0x7FFFFFFF and 0xFFFFFFFF (e.g. `struct{int lo,hi} x = {INT32_MAX,-1}`) as `mvni.2s v0,#0x80,lsl#24 ; fneg d0,d0 ; str d0,[x]` - a NaN bit pattern negated - which is a legal integer store ONLY because FNEG is a pure sign flip under AH=0. Under AH=1 the store silently writes {INT32_MAX,INT32_MAX}: a min/max accumulator armed that way never records a max. Detect: `mrs x0, fpcr` on the worker threads (bit 1), and the same binary's frame md5 differing between the two cores while a fresh-process probe of libm/NEON arithmetic (FPCR=0) is identical. Fix: write only the bits you mean (FZ, RMode).
- `94716a6a34db` trap **measure.min_of_n_interleaved_with_duplicate_arm** = "trap" (exact) {} w=1/5  [floating]  ‹global› — Perf A/B: interleave the arms, rotate order, take the min of N (11), and run a DUPLICATE of one arm as the contamination detector - a between-arm delta smaller than the duplicate's drift is not a finding. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from revival-fog]
- `acb4a44152f1` trap **measure.printf_hides_races** = "trap" (exact) {} w=1/5  [floating]  ‹global› — printf/stderr in a hot threaded path serialises the workers and HIDES the race you are hunting (heisenbug); record lock-free per-worker and dump at the end, or build-bisect. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from revival-fog]
- `948796f13635` trap **measure.two_run_md5_proves_nothing** = "trap" (exact) {} w=1/5  [floating]  ‹global› — A 2-run byte-identical gate cannot clear a threading race; determinism needs 24+ identical runs (md5 | sort | uniq -c) and a binomial before believing a clean batch (P(24 clean at 1-in-12) ~ 0.12). [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from revival-fog]
- `31d9206979ca` trap **shell.zsh.flag_string_splitting** = "trap" (exact) {shell=zsh} w=1/5  [floating]  ‹global› — zsh does not word-split an unquoted variable: a flag string in $VAR is one argument; use ${=VAR}. Also never echo a bare line of === in a script that feeds a parser. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from revival-fog]
- `3fccae10f485` trap **tool.pgrep.self_match** = "trap" (exact) {tool=pgrep} w=1/5  [floating]  ‹global› — pgrep -f matches the shell that runs it: box-quiet wait loops that grep their own command line deadlock on each other. Match executables (ps -Ao comm=), not argv. [seeded 2026-08-29 by the coordinator from revival-fog]
- `5fc35421cbaf` trap **zsh.batch_script.silent_no_op** = "trap" (exact) {project=revival-fog shell=zsh tool=zsh} w=2/5  [floating]  ‹global› — Two zsh idioms turn a batch script into a silent no-op. (1) rm -f a/*.x b/*.y aborts on NOMATCH before deleting ANYTHING when one pattern matches nothing, so a self-cleaning run keeps every file - setopt local_options null_glob. (2) An option string expanded as ${OPT} instead of ${=OPT} is passed as ONE argv word, so --bare /path reaches argparse as a single unrecognised argument and every invocation fails. Both were found only because the output tree was 1681 MiB instead of 250 and every score file held a usage message, not because anything errored.

## Laws and traps without supporting records (47)
Flagged, not shouted: they cite a document, not a measurement. Add `depends_on` naming the records they rest on; they move to Attention only when something current depends on them.

Apple M1/M2 lack FEAT_AFP - the bit is RES0 and the write is silently dropped - so code that sets it looks correct for years and then diverges on the first FEAT_AFP core (Apple M5). The specific bite: clang materialises a 64-bit integer pair whose lanes are 0x7FFFFFFF and 0xFFFFFFFF (e.g. `struct{int lo,hi} x = {INT32_MAX,-1}`) as `mvni.2s v0,#0x80,lsl#24 ; fneg d0,d0 ; str d0,[x]` - a NaN bit pattern negated - which is a legal integer store ONLY because FNEG is a pure sign flip under AH=0. Under AH=1 the store silently writes {INT32_MAX,INT32_MAX}: a min/max accumulator armed that way never records a max. - `804dba296145` law **detector.valid** = "valid" (state) {defect=wall detector=refdiff scene=greets} w=5/5 — refdiff (offline plane+heightfield reference, --refplane_dump + tools/refdiff_detect.py) is the validated wall-defect detector: exact 0.00 on the bare walls, fires on every era he labeled broken, orders them as he ordered them.
- `607c7cd2c810` trap **greets.displace.ref.fp_contract_backface** = "trap" (snapshot_md5) {flag=cull_front granularity=binary scene=greets tool=zsh} w=4/5 — Adding --cull_front as a TERNARY over two copies of the backface dot product moved BOTH greets pins WITH THE FLAG OFF: under -ffp-contract=fast the duplicated expression contracts differently and flips faces sitting exactly on the plane. Written as (test) != cullFront, leaving the float expression textually unchanged, all six pins are identical. Any flag that branches around a float comparison must not restate the comparison.
- `d26467f4645d` trap **greets.displace.ref.junction_pair_composite** = "holds" (state) {defect=wall detector=refdiff scene=greets} w=3/5 — docs/img/refrender/junction_H6194.png and junction_corner6097.png (the corner_rule ruling images) are NOT the two arms side by side: their right half is 2.4x darker than the left EVERYWHERE (mean R 111.8 vs 46.3 in a block region 250 px from the crease) and carries a crease-overlay tint, while fresh renders of the two arms have identical mean RGB (111.1 / 101.4 / 76.8 at H6194, 106.8 / 96.3 / 73.7 at corner6097). His eye caught it: one side is way brighter, and that is the composite, not the geometry. Trap: a comparison picture composed by an agent is not the render - re-render both arms with one flag apart and cut the same pixels before putting a pair in front of him. The rev-2 deck does that.
- `60e3e63bed65` trap **greets.displace.ref.smooth_seam_margin** = "trap" (state) {defect=wall flag=greets_displace_ref_margin scene=greets tool=DisplaceStoneSubdiv} w=3/5 — cot(phi/2) is the BISECTOR partition requirement, not every edge requirement. Sizing a SMOOTH seam by it (cot 5 deg = 11.4) replaced the curved wall with its tangent planes extended a whole unit, each strip standing proud of the curve by the sagitta and the pair mutually satisfying each other mitre trim. A crease is a corner and needs max(cot,tan)(phi/2); a smooth seam is one surface and needs only the mitre tan(phi/2). Max margin 1.059 -> 0.578 u.
- `80a9fd84d55d` trap **greets.displace.v4.trap.flip_rate_instrument** = "instrument_blind_to_v4" (state) {detector=flip_rate scene=greets state=P3b tool=flip_rate} w=3/5 — tools/flip_rate.sh CANNOT measure a v4 arm: its greets recipe omits --greets-displace, so --greets_displace_v4 is inert and 24 runs reproduce the OFF-arm pin no matter what arm flags you append
- `ba7c2e825b1f` trap **greets.render.mip_is_per_polygon** = 265108 (count) {granularity=whole_frame metric=miplevel scene=greets} w=3/5 — THE ALBEDO MIP IS A FUNCTION OF THE TESSELLATION, NOT ONLY OF THE CAMERA. FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP:945 picks the miplevel per CLIPPED POLYGON: under MinSize = XRes*YRes*0.02 pixels (41472 at 1920x1080) one mip is taken for the whole polygon from 0.5*log2(texArea*SizeX*SizeY/pixArea); above it the mipmap-via-subdivision path splits the polygon and each piece gets its own. So subdividing a wall changes which mip its pixels sample even when every new vertex lies exactly on the authored plane: 265108 pixels change mip (0->1 on 225884, 0->2 on 39224) between the 226 authored stone triangles and a 60606-triangle UNDISPLACED lattice of the same planes at greets cam A, and the SHIPPED bake at amplitude 0 shows the same signature. Consequence for any 'byte-identical after retessellation' plan: it cannot hold. Consequence for any A/B whose arms differ in face count: the colour delta is not attributable to the shading change under test until the mip plane is differenced -- the G-buffer packs it in bits 28..31 of the mat32 word, dumpable with FDS_SNAPSHOT_GBUFDUMP and read by tools/v4_p2_planes.py.
- `015ae8c8e985` trap **measure.concurrent_merge_contaminated_arm** = "trap" (state) {state=rwdi_test_2026_08_30 tool=m5} w=1/5 — The dev-box RelWithDebInfo ceiling test was CONTAMINATED by my own concurrent merge: the GTAO-defaults merge landed on fog-wt between the Release reference render and the RWDI build, so the RWDI arm silently carried the NEW AO defaults and the 96%-of-pixels difference measured the flag flip, not -O2. The only valid comparison from that batch remains M5 vs the pre-merge local Release (24.79%, the ceiling block). Rule: an A/B across builds must pin the SOURCE SHA of both arms (detached worktree at a fixed commit), never build from a branch another task is advancing. The provenance sidecars carry the SHA - read them before diffing.

- `ccd53bb80c90` trap **tool.cmake.language_cxx_on_mm** = "trap" (state) {tool=cmake} w=1/5 — set_source_files_properties(... LANGUAGE CXX) on Objective-C++ .mm sources emits an explicit -x c++ since CMake CMP0119 (>=3.20; NEW by default with a fresh 4.x cache), overriding the .mm extension: clang warns argument unused for -ObjC++ and then rejects every @class in the Foundation headers. Bit the M5 (fresh CLT + fresh cache) while the dev box coasted on an old cache. Fix: enable_language(OBJCXX) in the gated subdirectory and drop the LANGUAGE override; -fobjc-arc stays as a per-file option. Verified: GpuBench configures and links on the dev box with the fix.
- `1c446f7c1625` trap **tool.groundwork.amend_orphans_head** = "trap" (state) {tool=groundwork} w=1/5 — Records auto-capture the repo HEAD at write time. Writing records and then AMENDING (or rebasing away) that commit leaves their head pointing at a commit that is no longer an ancestor of the tip, so the tool treats them as unmerged-branch records: they can neither re-verify anything nor count as at-tip. Happened 2026-08-30: four agreeing render_gate rows written at f02e31b4, commit amended to 5381c68b, the four baselines stayed unverified (36 instead of 32). Rule: write records, then make a NEW commit; never amend/rebase a commit that records were written against without re-filing them.
- `3fccae10f485` trap **tool.pgrep.self_match** = "trap" (exact) {tool=pgrep} w=1/5  ‹global› — pgrep -f matches the shell that runs it: box-quiet wait loops that grep their own command line deadlock on each other. - `7cc7de708c04` trap **tool.snapshot.resolution_follows_rev_cfg** = "holds" (state) {tool=snapshot} w=3/5 — Every --snapshot row that does not pass --force_xres/--force_yres renders at Runtime/rev.cfg resolution, and the binary finds rev.cfg by walking up from the REAL executable path (DEMO/REV.CPP lines 526-594: first directory with a rev.cfg wins, then chdir) - a symlinked DEMO in another directory with its own rev.cfg still reads Runtime/rev.cfg. On 2026-09-01 the dev box rev.cfg was locally modified to 1384x768 (uncommitted), so tools/ovec/gates.sh read 0 PASS / 12 FAIL twice (PPM headers P6 1384 768) with NO pixel change in the tree: the greets t=1588 row re-run with --force_xres=1920 --force_yres=1080 matches its pin 570a7b443f768393dc6647044a9e67b3 exactly. tools/refrender_battery.sh has the same dependence (its 2026-09-01 renders are 1384x768). Before reading any md5 battery: check git diff Runtime/rev.cfg, or force the resolution on the row.
- `609d12df45a9` trap **trap.miplevel_minsize_frozen_static** = 5242.88 (count) {metric=mip_minsize pose=camA_t5965 scene=greets tool=m5} w=3/5 — MiplevelClipper (FDS/FRUSTRUM/FRUSTRUM.CPP) computes its small-face threshold as `static const float MinSize = XRes*YRes * 0.02;` - a FUNCTION-LOCAL static, initialised ONCE by whichever thread calls first, from whatever the XRes/YRes globals held then. MEASURED at greets cam A on a 1920x1080 run: MinSize = 5242.88, i.e. 512x512 - an offscreen bake got there first, so the MAIN view runs the whole process with a threshold 7.9x too small (it should be 41472). The source comment ("update if XRes and/or YRes changes...") documents the bug and nothing does it. Consequences: the poly-split path is entered for faces meant to take the uniform-mip fast path, the value silently depends on WHICH pass touches the clipper first (a scheduling property), and a window resize never updates it. NOT the cause of the M5 divergence - both branches still rasterise the face, verified - but a live resolution-dependence trap for anyone reading mip behaviour or pricing the split path.
- `5fc35421cbaf` trap **zsh.batch_script.silent_no_op** = "trap" (exact) {project=revival-fog shell=zsh tool=zsh} w=2/5  ‹global› — Two zsh idioms turn a batch script into a silent no-op. (1) rm -f a/*.x b/*.y aborts on NOMATCH before deleting ANYTHING when one pattern matches nothing, so a self-cleaning run keeps every file - setopt local_options null_glob. (2) An option string expanded as ${OPT} instead of ${=OPT} is passed as ONE argv word, so --bare /path reaches argparse as a single unrecognised argument and every invocation fails. Both were found only because the output tree was 1681 MiB instead of 250 and every score file held a usage message, not because anything errored.
